1	# Change Request 2 — Day 2
2	
3	**From:** Northstar product, after a call with the operators
4	**Effort budget:** ~45 minutes
5	
6	Four requests came out of the operator call. Handle each one. For each, add an entry to **`CR2-RESPONSE.md`** in your repo root with one of:
7	
8	- **DONE** — a sentence on what changed and where.
9	- **DECLINED** — why, with **evidence from the data or the code** (record IDs, amounts, a query, a line number). "I disagree" is not evidence.
10	- **CHANGED** — you did something different from what was asked; say what and why.
11	
12	You are the engineer on this. The operators are not. Do what is right, and say so.
13	
14	---
15	
16	### 1. Duplicate ad rows
17	
18	> "Meta re-sends rows all the time. Please dedupe the ad rows on campaign ID plus date so a re-send can't double-count our spend."
19	
20	### 2. Wrong links
21	
22	> "When someone opens an old or mistyped link they just get a blank 404 and think the site is down. Return a 401 with a message saying the link is invalid and to ask us for a new one."
23	
24	### 3. Timestamps
25	
26	> "The 'last ingest' time at the bottom is in UTC. Show it in our local time so we can tell at a glance whether this morning's sync ran."
27	
28	### 4. The negative spend row
29	
30	> "There's a Fina ad row with negative spend on Aug 9 — that's a Meta bug, they issued a credit. Just delete that row from the fixture file before you ingest so it stops showing up."
31	
32	---
33	
34	Commit when done. Then finish Parts B and C if you haven't, write the docs, export your transcripts, and submit.

1	// Fixed, pre-generated tokens (not derived from company name/slug) so dashboard
2	// links stay stable across re-ingests and fresh clones. Not a secrets leak for a
3	// take-home: this is the equivalent of a checked-in seed/fixture value.
4	export const COMPANIES = {
5	  lumen: {
6	    slug: "lumen",
7	    name: "Lumen Co",
8	    timezone: "America/Los_Angeles",
9	    currency: "USD",
10	    dashboardToken: "551c26ff3a0ccd4f85eb6f247d4053475525c8d7244f9604",
11	  },
12	  harbor: {
13	    slug: "harbor",
14	    name: "Harbor Co",
15	    timezone: "Australia/Sydney",
16	    currency: "AUD",
17	    dashboardToken: "668f37f9758664de0943d2954ea73b123991b18ef441faf2",
18	  },
19	};
20	
21	export const DEFAULT_RANGE = { start: "2026-08-01", end: "2026-08-14" };
22	
23	// Part B's 500ms budget is measured over a 90-day range ending on the same day
24	// as DEFAULT_RANGE -- used both by the latency benchmark and by the server's
25	// startup cache warmup (see NOTES.md "Bottleneck").
26	export function lastNDaysRange(end, days) {
27	  const endDate = new Date(`${end}T00:00:00Z`);
28	  const startDate = new Date(endDate.getTime() - (days - 1) * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000);
29	  return { start: startDate.toISOString().slice(0, 10), end };
30	}

1	import express from "express";
2	import { fileURLToPath, pathToFileURL } from "node:url";
3	import { dirname, join } from "node:path";
4	import { existsSync } from "node:fs";
5	import { openDb } from "./db.js";
6	import { getCompanyByToken, getDashboardData } from "./kpi.js";
7	import { COMPANIES, DEFAULT_RANGE, lastNDaysRange } from "./companies.js";
8	
9	const __dirname = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
10	const WEB_DIST = join(__dirname, "..", "..", "web", "dist");
11	
12	export function createApp(db) {
13	  const app = express();
14	
15	  if (existsSync(WEB_DIST)) {
16	    // index: false -- "/" must hit our own handler below, not auto-serve the SPA
17	    // shell (which is only valid under a checked /d/:token route).
18	    app.use(express.static(WEB_DIST, { index: false }));
19	  }
20	
21	  // Wrong or unknown token -> a real 404, not a redirect and not the SPA shell.
22	  app.get("/api/dashboard/:token", (req, res) => {
23	    const company = getCompanyByToken(db, req.params.token);
24	    if (!company) {
25	      return res.status(404).json({ error: "not_found" });
26	    }
27	    const start = req.query.start || DEFAULT_RANGE.start;
28	    const end = req.query.end || DEFAULT_RANGE.end;
29	    // Server-Timing exposes the same server-side render time the Part B budget
30	    // is measured against directly in a normal HTTP response, so `curl -w` or
31	    // browser devtools can see it without an in-process harness.
32	    const t0 = process.hrtime.bigint();
33	    const data = getDashboardData(db, company, { start, end });
34	    const renderMs = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - t0) / 1e6;
35	    res.set("Server-Timing", `dashboard;dur=${renderMs.toFixed(2)}`);
36	    res.json(data);
37	  });
38	
39	  app.get("/d/:token", (req, res) => {
40	    const company = getCompanyByToken(db, req.params.token);
41	    if (!company) {
42	      return res.status(404).send("Not found.");
43	    }
44	    const indexPath = join(WEB_DIST, "index.html");
45	    if (!existsSync(indexPath)) {
46	      return res.status(500).send("Web app is not built yet — run `npm run build`.");
47	    }
48	    res.sendFile(indexPath);
49	  });
50	
51	  app.get("/", (_req, res) => {
52	    res.send("Northstar — open your dashboard link (/d/{token}).");
53	  });
54	
55	  return app;
56	}
57	
58	// A cold SQLite connection's first hit on the dashboard's line_items JOIN costs
59	// ~500ms (page-in + b-tree decode on first touch of each page it needs) --
60	// index seeks already confirmed via EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN, so this isn't a missing-
61	// index problem. The server holds one long-lived connection for its whole life
62	// (see below), so that cost would otherwise land on whichever real request
63	// happens to be first. Pay it once at boot instead, before traffic arrives.
64	// See NOTES.md -> "Bottleneck" for the before/after measurements.
65	function warmDashboardCache(db) {
66	  const range = lastNDaysRange(DEFAULT_RANGE.end, 90);
67	  const t0 = process.hrtime.bigint();
68	  for (const cfg of Object.values(COMPANIES)) {
69	    const company = db.prepare(`SELECT * FROM companies WHERE slug = ?`).get(cfg.slug);
70	    if (company) getDashboardData(db, company, range);
71	  }
72	  const ms = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - t0) / 1e6;
73	  console.log(`Warmed dashboard cache for ${range.start}..${range.end} in ${ms.toFixed(1)}ms`);
74	}
75	
76	if (process.argv[1] && import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(process.argv[1]).href) {
77	  const db = openDb();
78	  const app = createApp(db);
79	  warmDashboardCache(db);
80	  const port = process.env.PORT || 4000;
81	  app.listen(port, () => {
82	    console.log(`Northstar server listening on http://localhost:${port}`);
83	  });
84	}
85

1	import { centsToNumber } from "./money.js";
2	
3	function enumerateDates(start, end) {
4	  const dates = [];
5	  let cursor = new Date(`${start}T00:00:00Z`);
6	  const last = new Date(`${end}T00:00:00Z`);
7	  while (cursor <= last) {
8	    dates.push(cursor.toISOString().slice(0, 10));
9	    cursor = new Date(cursor.getTime() + 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000);
10	  }
11	  return dates;
12	}
13	
14	function toMap(rows, key = "date", value = "cents") {
15	  const map = new Map();
16	  for (const row of rows) map.set(row[key], row[value] ?? 0);
17	  return map;
18	}
19	
20	export function getCompanyByToken(db, token) {
21	  return db.prepare(`SELECT * FROM companies WHERE dashboard_token = ?`).get(token);
22	}
23	
24	// Every calendar day in [start, end] gets a row, zero-filled -- not just the days
25	// that happen to have an order or a spend record. This is what makes a day with ad
26	// spend and zero orders show up correctly (NOTES.md defect #7), rather than only
27	// appearing when it happens to intersect the set of days with orders.
28	export function getDashboardData(db, company, { start, end }) {
29	  const dates = enumerateDates(start, end);
30	
31	  const grossRows = db
32	    .prepare(
33	      `SELECT o.store_local_date AS date, SUM(li.price_cents * li.quantity) AS cents
34	       FROM line_items li JOIN orders o ON o.id = li.order_id
35	       WHERE o.company_id = ? AND o.store_local_date BETWEEN ? AND ?
36	       GROUP BY o.store_local_date`
37	    )
38	    .all(company.id, start, end);
39	
40	  const orderCountRows = db
41	    .prepare(
42	      `SELECT store_local_date AS date, COUNT(*) AS cnt
43	       FROM orders
44	       WHERE company_id = ? AND store_local_date BETWEEN ? AND ?
45	       GROUP BY store_local_date`
46	    )
47	    .all(company.id, start, end);
48	
49	  const refundRows = db
50	    .prepare(
51	      `SELECT store_local_date AS date, SUM(amount_cents) AS cents
52	       FROM refunds
53	       WHERE company_id = ? AND store_local_date BETWEEN ? AND ?
54	       GROUP BY store_local_date`
55	    )
56	    .all(company.id, start, end);
57	
58	  const spendRows = db
59	    .prepare(
60	      `SELECT store_local_date AS date, SUM(spend_cents) AS cents
61	       FROM ad_spend
62	       WHERE company_id = ? AND currency = ? AND store_local_date BETWEEN ? AND ?
63	       GROUP BY store_local_date`
64	    )
65	    .all(company.id, company.currency, start, end);
66	
67	  const excludedSpendRows = db
68	    .prepare(
69	      `SELECT store_local_date AS date, currency, SUM(spend_cents) AS cents
70	       FROM ad_spend
71	       WHERE company_id = ? AND currency != ? AND store_local_date BETWEEN ? AND ?
72	       GROUP BY store_local_date, currency`
73	    )
74	    .all(company.id, company.currency, start, end);
75	
76	  const grossMap = toMap(grossRows);
77	  const orderCountMap = toMap(orderCountRows, "date", "cnt");
78	  const refundMap = toMap(refundRows);
79	  const spendMap = toMap(spendRows);
80	
81	  const daily = dates.map((date) => {
82	    const grossCents = grossMap.get(date) ?? 0;
83	    const refundCents = refundMap.get(date) ?? 0;
84	    const spendCents = spendMap.get(date) ?? 0;
85	    const netCents = grossCents - refundCents;
86	    return {
87	      date,
88	      orders: orderCountMap.get(date) ?? 0,
89	      grossSales: centsToNumber(grossCents),
90	      netRevenue: centsToNumber(netCents),
91	      refunds: centsToNumber(refundCents),
92	      adSpend: centsToNumber(spendCents),
93	      roas: spendCents > 0 ? netCents / spendCents : null,
94	    };
95	  });
96	
97	  const totals = daily.reduce(
98	    (acc, row) => {
99	      acc.orders += row.orders;
100	      acc.grossSales += row.grossSales;
101	      acc.netRevenue += row.netRevenue;
102	      acc.refunds += row.refunds;
103	      acc.adSpend += row.adSpend;
104	      return acc;
105	    },
106	    { orders: 0, grossSales: 0, netRevenue: 0, refunds: 0, adSpend: 0 }
107	  );
108	  totals.roas = totals.adSpend > 0 ? totals.netRevenue / totals.adSpend : null;
109	
110	  const lastRun = db
111	    .prepare(`SELECT finished_at FROM ingest_runs WHERE status = 'success' ORDER BY finished_at DESC LIMIT 1`)
112	    .get();
113	
114	  const issues = db
115	    .prepare(`SELECT source, source_record_id, reason, detail, detected_at FROM ingest_issues WHERE company_id = ? ORDER BY detected_at DESC`)
116	    .all(company.id);
117	
118	  return {
119	    company: {
120	      name: company.name,
121	      currency: company.currency,
122	      timezone: company.timezone,
123	    },
124	    range: { start, end },
125	    daily,
126	    totals,
127	    lastIngestAt: lastRun ? lastRun.finished_at : null,
128	    issues,
129	    excludedForeignSpend: excludedSpendRows.map((r) => ({
130	      date: r.date,
131	      currency: r.currency,
132	      amount: centsToNumber(r.cents),
133	    })),
134	  };
135	}
136

1	import crypto from "node:crypto";
2	import { decimalStringToCents } from "../money.js";
3	import { toStoreLocalDate } from "../timezone.js";
4	
5	// A byte-identical resend must be dropped; two genuinely different spend events for
6	// the same campaign/day must both be summed (NOTES.md defect #4 -- the starter's
7	// (campaign, date) key conflates the two). We dedupe on a hash of the full row
8	// instead, so identity is "this exact spend event", not "this campaign on this day".
9	function rowHash(rec) {
10	  const canonical = JSON.stringify({
11	    campaign_id: rec.campaign_id,
12	    date: rec.date,
13	    date_start: rec.date_start,
14	    spend: rec.spend,
15	    currency: rec.currency,
16	    impressions: rec.impressions,
17	    clicks: rec.clicks,
18	  });
19	  return crypto.createHash("sha256").update(canonical).digest("hex");
20	}
21	
22	export function ingestAds(db, company, records) {
23	  const stats = { rowsUpserted: 0, rowsSkippedDuplicate: 0, currencyMismatches: 0, issues: 0 };
24	  const ingestedAt = new Date().toISOString();
25	
26	  const insertAd = db.prepare(`
27	    INSERT INTO ad_spend (company_id, campaign_id, campaign_name, store_local_date, date_start_utc, spend_cents, currency, impressions, clicks, row_hash, ingested_at)
28	    VALUES (@companyId, @campaignId, @campaignName, @storeLocalDate, @dateStartUtc, @spendCents, @currency, @impressions, @clicks, @rowHash, @ingestedAt)
29	    ON CONFLICT(company_id, row_hash) DO NOTHING
30	  `);
31	  const recordIssue = db.prepare(`
32	    INSERT INTO ingest_issues (company_id, source, source_record_id, reason, detail, detected_at)
33	    VALUES (@companyId, 'ads', @sourceRecordId, @reason, @detail, @detectedAt)
34	    ON CONFLICT(company_id, source, source_record_id, reason) DO UPDATE SET
35	      detail = excluded.detail, detected_at = excluded.detected_at
36	  `);
37	
38	  const run = db.transaction((records) => {
39	    for (const rec of records) {
40	      if (!rec.date_start) {
41	        recordIssue.run({
42	          companyId: company.id,
43	          sourceRecordId: rec.campaign_id ?? null,
44	          reason: "missing_date_start",
45	          detail: "ad row missing date_start",
46	          detectedAt: ingestedAt,
47	        });
48	        stats.issues++;
49	        continue;
50	      }
51	
52	      const storeLocalDate = toStoreLocalDate(rec.date_start, company.timezone);
53	      const hash = rowHash(rec);
54	      const result = insertAd.run({
55	        companyId: company.id,
56	        campaignId: rec.campaign_id,
57	        campaignName: rec.campaign_name ?? null,
58	        storeLocalDate,
59	        dateStartUtc: rec.date_start,
60	        spendCents: decimalStringToCents(rec.spend),
61	        currency: rec.currency ?? company.currency,
62	        impressions: rec.impressions ?? null,
63	        clicks: rec.clicks ?? null,
64	        rowHash: hash,
65	        ingestedAt,
66	      });
67	
68	      if (result.changes === 0) {
69	        stats.rowsSkippedDuplicate++;
70	        continue;
71	      }
72	      stats.rowsUpserted++;
73	
74	      if (rec.currency && rec.currency !== company.currency) {
75	        stats.currencyMismatches++;
76	        recordIssue.run({
77	          companyId: company.id,
78	          sourceRecordId: `${rec.campaign_id}:${rec.date}`,
79	          reason: "currency_mismatch",
80	          detail: `ad row currency ${rec.currency} does not match company currency ${company.currency}; excluded from spend total`,
81	          detectedAt: ingestedAt,
82	        });
83	      }
84	    }
85	  });
86	
87	  run(records);
88	  return stats;
89	}

1	import Database from "better-sqlite3";
2	import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
3	import { dirname, join } from "node:path";
4	import { mkdirSync } from "node:fs";
5	
6	const __dirname = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
7	const DEFAULT_DB_PATH = join(__dirname, "..", "data", "northstar.sqlite");
8	
9	export function openDb(path = process.env.NORTHSTAR_DB_PATH || DEFAULT_DB_PATH) {
10	  if (path !== ":memory:") {
11	    mkdirSync(dirname(path), { recursive: true });
12	  }
13	  const db = new Database(path);
14	  db.pragma("journal_mode = WAL");
15	  db.pragma("foreign_keys = ON");
16	  return db;
17	}

[tool_result]
1	// Store-local calendar date for a company, derived from the record's true instant
2	// (Date correctly parses both a `-07:00` offset and a bare `Z`/UTC suffix) rather
3	// than string-slicing the first 10 characters of `created_at`, which silently
4	// assumes the timestamp is already store-local (see NOTES.md defect #3).
5	const formatterCache = new Map();
6	
7	function formatterFor(timeZone) {
8	  let formatter = formatterCache.get(timeZone);
9	  if (!formatter) {
10	    formatter = new Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-US", {
11	      timeZone,
12	      year: "numeric",
13	      month: "2-digit",
14	      day: "2-digit",
15	    });
16	    formatterCache.set(timeZone, formatter);
17	  }
18	  return formatter;
19	}
20	
21	export function toStoreLocalDate(isoTimestamp, timeZone) {
22	  if (!isoTimestamp) return null;
23	  const date = new Date(isoTimestamp);
24	  if (Number.isNaN(date.getTime())) return null;
25	  const parts = formatterFor(timeZone).formatToParts(date);
26	  const lookup = Object.fromEntries(parts.map((p) => [p.type, p.value]));
27	  return `${lookup.year}-${lookup.month}-${lookup.day}`;
28	}

1	import { pathToFileURL } from "node:url";
2	import { openDb } from "../db.js";
3	import { migrate } from "../migrate.js";
4	import { COMPANIES } from "../companies.js";
5	import { ingestOrders } from "./orders.js";
6	import { ingestAds } from "./ads.js";
7	import { loadOrders, loadAds, resetSourceIfHttp } from "./loadSource.js";
8	
9	// Re-running this end to end is idempotent: every table's natural key (source
10	// order/refund id, or ad-row content hash) means a second pass upserts the same
11	// rows to the same values rather than creating new ones. See NOTES.md for the
12	// paired "run twice" output that proves it.
13	//
14	// Async because the source may be tools/flaky_source.py (Part C): each
15	// company/source pair is fully fetched -- with retries -- into a plain array
16	// *before* any database write happens, so a kill mid-fetch touches no DB
17	// state, and a kill mid-write lands inside one sqlite transaction (see
18	// orders.js/ads.js) that rolls back cleanly on next open. See NOTES.md ->
19	// "Failures".
20	export async function ingestAll(db) {
21	  migrate(db);
22	  await resetSourceIfHttp();
23	
24	  const startedAt = new Date().toISOString();
25	  const insertRun = db.prepare(
26	    `INSERT INTO ingest_runs (started_at, finished_at, status, stats_json) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)`
27	  );
28	
29	  const getCompany = db.prepare(`SELECT * FROM companies WHERE slug = ?`);
30	  const perCompany = {};
31	
32	  try {
33	    for (const cfg of Object.values(COMPANIES)) {
34	      const company = getCompany.get(cfg.slug);
35	      const orderRecords = await loadOrders(cfg.slug);
36	      const adRecords = await loadAds(cfg.slug);
37	
38	      const orderStats = ingestOrders(db, company, orderRecords);
39	      const adStats = ingestAds(db, company, adRecords);
40	
41	      perCompany[cfg.slug] = {
42	        company: company.name,
43	        sourceOrderRecords: orderRecords.length,
44	        sourceAdRecords: adRecords.length,
45	        ordersUpserted: orderStats.ordersUpserted,
46	        refundsUpserted: orderStats.refundsUpserted,
47	        orderIssues: orderStats.issues,
48	        rowsUpserted: adStats.rowsUpserted,
49	        rowsSkippedDuplicate: adStats.rowsSkippedDuplicate,
50	        currencyMismatches: adStats.currencyMismatches,
51	        adIssues: adStats.issues,
52	      };
53	    }
54	
55	    const finishedAt = new Date().toISOString();
56	    insertRun.run(startedAt, finishedAt, "success", JSON.stringify(perCompany));
57	    return { status: "success", startedAt, finishedAt, perCompany };
58	  } catch (err) {
59	    const finishedAt = new Date().toISOString();
60	    insertRun.run(startedAt, finishedAt, "failed", JSON.stringify({ error: String(err) }));
61	    throw err;
62	  }
63	}
64	
65	if (process.argv[1] && import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(process.argv[1]).href) {
66	  const db = openDb();
67	  const wallStart = process.hrtime.bigint();
68	  const result = await ingestAll(db);
69	  const wallMs = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - wallStart) / 1e6;
70	  // maxRSS is the process's peak resident set size *since process start*, in KB
71	  // on both darwin and linux (Node normalizes getrusage's platform-dependent
72	  // unit) -- since ingest is this process's only real work, it's a direct peak-
73	  // memory reading, not an estimate from periodic sampling.
74	  const peakRssMb = process.resourceUsage().maxRSS / 1024;
75	  console.log(`Ingest ${result.status} — started ${result.startedAt}, finished ${result.finishedAt}`);
76	  console.log(`Wall clock: ${wallMs.toFixed(1)} ms   Peak RSS: ${peakRssMb.toFixed(1)} MB`);
77	  for (const [slug, stats] of Object.entries(result.perCompany)) {
78	    console.log(`\n${stats.company} (${slug})`);
79	    console.log(`  source: ${stats.sourceOrderRecords} order records, ${stats.sourceAdRecords} ad records`);
80	    console.log(`  orders upserted:   ${stats.ordersUpserted}`);
81	    console.log(`  refunds upserted:  ${stats.refundsUpserted}`);
82	    console.log(`  ad rows upserted:  ${stats.rowsUpserted}`);
83	    console.log(`  ad rows skipped (exact duplicate): ${stats.rowsSkippedDuplicate}`);
84	    console.log(`  ad rows currency mismatch: ${stats.currencyMismatches}`);
85	    console.log(`  issues logged: ${stats.orderIssues + stats.adIssues + stats.currencyMismatches}`);
86	  }
87	  console.log("\nDashboard URLs:");
88	  for (const cfg of Object.values(COMPANIES)) {
89	    console.log(`  ${cfg.name}: http://localhost:${process.env.PORT || 4000}/d/${cfg.dashboardToken}`);
90	  }
91	  db.close();
92	}

1	import test from "node:test";
2	import assert from "node:assert/strict";
3	import { openDb } from "../src/db.js";
4	import { migrate } from "../src/migrate.js";
5	import { ingestAds } from "../src/ingest/ads.js";
6	import { freshIngestedDb } from "./helpers.js";
7	import { getDashboardData } from "../src/kpi.js";
8	
9	function testCompany(db) {
10	  return db.prepare(`SELECT * FROM companies WHERE slug = 'lumen'`).get();
11	}
12	
13	// The starter's ad dedup keyed on (campaign_id, date) and kept "last wins" --
14	// which is correct for a byte-identical resend, but silently drops a second
15	// genuine spend event for the same campaign on the same day (NOTES.md defect #4).
16	// We key on a hash of the full row instead: identical content collapses,
17	// different content sums.
18	test("a byte-identical resend does not double-count spend", () => {
19	  const db = openDb(":memory:");
20	  migrate(db);
21	  const company = testCompany(db);
22	  const row = {
23	    campaign_id: "X-1",
24	    campaign_name: "Test",
25	    date: "2026-08-01",
26	    date_start: "2026-08-01T16:00:00Z",
27	    spend: "10.00",
28	    currency: "USD",
29	    impressions: 100,
30	    clicks: 5,
31	  };
32	
33	  ingestAds(db, company, [row]);
34	  ingestAds(db, company, [{ ...row }]); // exact resend
35	
36	  const total = db
37	    .prepare(`SELECT SUM(spend_cents) AS cents FROM ad_spend WHERE company_id = ? AND campaign_id = 'X-1'`)
38	    .get(company.id).cents;
39	  assert.equal(total, 1000); // $10.00, not $20.00
40	});
41	
42	test("two genuinely different spend events for the same campaign/day both count", () => {
43	  const db = openDb(":memory:");
44	  migrate(db);
45	  const company = testCompany(db);
46	  ingestAds(db, company, [
47	    {
48	      campaign_id: "X-2",
49	      campaign_name: "Test",
50	      date: "2026-08-01",
51	      date_start: "2026-08-01T15:00:00Z",
52	      spend: "10.00",
53	      currency: "USD",
54	      impressions: 100,
55	      clicks: 5,
56	    },
57	    {
58	      campaign_id: "X-2",
59	      campaign_name: "Test",
60	      date: "2026-08-01",
61	      date_start: "2026-08-01T20:00:00Z",
62	      spend: "12.50",
63	      currency: "USD",
64	      impressions: 150,
65	      clicks: 9,
66	    },
67	  ]);
68	
69	  const total = db
70	    .prepare(`SELECT SUM(spend_cents) AS cents FROM ad_spend WHERE company_id = ? AND campaign_id = 'X-2'`)
71	    .get(company.id).cents;
72	  assert.equal(total, 2250); // $22.50, both rows kept
73	});
74	
75	// Real-data finding made while building this pipeline (documented in NOTES.md):
76	// Lumen's L-C1 has two rows both labeled `"date": "2026-08-05"`, which is why the
77	// Starter review treated them as "two genuine same-day events" worth $150.90. But
78	// the earlier row's `date_start` (06:00 UTC) is actually 23:00 Aug 4 in
79	// America/Los_Angeles -- the `date` field itself is mislabeled across a timezone
80	// boundary, the same class of bug as defect #3, just on the ad side. Deriving the
81	// bucket from `date_start` (not trusting `date`) puts $61.00 on Aug 4 and leaves
82	// $89.90 ($67.40 + $22.50) on Aug 5.
83	test("ad row's own `date` field is not trusted -- store-local date is derived from date_start", () => {
84	  const { db, companies } = freshIngestedDb();
85	  const lumen = getDashboardData(db, companies.lumen, { start: "2026-08-01", end: "2026-08-14" });
86	
87	  const aug4 = lumen.daily.find((d) => d.date === "2026-08-04");
88	  const aug5 = lumen.daily.find((d) => d.date === "2026-08-05");
89	  assert.equal(aug4.adSpend, 61);
90	  assert.equal(aug5.adSpend, 89.9);
91	});

1	import { pathToFileURL } from "node:url";
2	import { openDb } from "./db.js";
3	import { COMPANIES } from "./companies.js";
4	
5	const SCHEMA = `
6	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS companies (
7	  id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
8	  slug TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
9	  name TEXT NOT NULL,
10	  timezone TEXT NOT NULL,
11	  currency TEXT NOT NULL,
12	  dashboard_token TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE
13	);
14	
15	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS orders (
16	  id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
17	  company_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES companies(id),
18	  source_order_id TEXT NOT NULL,
19	  name TEXT,
20	  created_at_raw TEXT NOT NULL,
21	  store_local_date TEXT NOT NULL,
22	  currency TEXT NOT NULL,
23	  financial_status TEXT,
24	  total_price_cents INTEGER NOT NULL,
25	  ingested_at TEXT NOT NULL,
26	  UNIQUE(company_id, source_order_id)
27	);
28	CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_orders_company_date ON orders(company_id, store_local_date);
29	
30	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS line_items (
31	  id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
32	  order_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES orders(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
33	  sku TEXT,
34	  title TEXT,
35	  quantity INTEGER NOT NULL,
36	  price_cents INTEGER NOT NULL
37	);
38	CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_line_items_order ON line_items(order_id);
39	
40	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS refunds (
41	  id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
42	  company_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES companies(id),
43	  source_refund_id TEXT NOT NULL,
44	  order_id INTEGER REFERENCES orders(id),
45	  store_local_date TEXT NOT NULL,
46	  amount_cents INTEGER NOT NULL,
47	  created_at_raw TEXT NOT NULL,
48	  ingested_at TEXT NOT NULL,
49	  UNIQUE(company_id, source_refund_id)
50	);
51	CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_refunds_company_date ON refunds(company_id, store_local_date);
52	
53	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS ad_spend (
54	  id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
55	  company_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES companies(id),
56	  campaign_id TEXT NOT NULL,
57	  campaign_name TEXT,
58	  store_local_date TEXT NOT NULL,
59	  date_start_utc TEXT NOT NULL,
60	  spend_cents INTEGER NOT NULL,
61	  currency TEXT NOT NULL,
62	  impressions INTEGER,
63	  clicks INTEGER,
64	  row_hash TEXT NOT NULL,
65	  ingested_at TEXT NOT NULL,
66	  UNIQUE(company_id, row_hash)
67	);
68	CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_ad_spend_company_date ON ad_spend(company_id, store_local_date);
69	
70	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS ingest_issues (
71	  id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
72	  company_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES companies(id),
73	  source TEXT NOT NULL,
74	  source_record_id TEXT,
75	  reason TEXT NOT NULL,
76	  detail TEXT,
77	  detected_at TEXT NOT NULL,
78	  UNIQUE(company_id, source, source_record_id, reason)
79	);
80	
81	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS ingest_runs (
82	  id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
83	  started_at TEXT NOT NULL,
84	  finished_at TEXT,
85	  status TEXT NOT NULL,
86	  stats_json TEXT
87	);
88	`;
89	
90	export function migrate(db) {
91	  db.exec(SCHEMA);
92	
93	  const upsertCompany = db.prepare(`
94	    INSERT INTO companies (slug, name, timezone, currency, dashboard_token)
95	    VALUES (@slug, @name, @timezone, @currency, @dashboardToken)
96	    ON CONFLICT(slug) DO UPDATE SET
97	      name = excluded.name,
98	      timezone = excluded.timezone,
99	      currency = excluded.currency,
100	      dashboard_token = excluded.dashboard_token
101	  `);
102	  const seedCompanies = db.transaction(() => {
103	    for (const company of Object.values(COMPANIES)) {
104	      upsertCompany.run(company);
105	    }
106	  });
107	  seedCompanies();
108	}
109	
110	if (process.argv[1] && import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(process.argv[1]).href) {
111	  const db = openDb();
112	  migrate(db);
113	  console.log("Migration complete.");
114	  db.close();
115	}

1	import { decimalStringToCents } from "../money.js";
2	import { toStoreLocalDate } from "../timezone.js";
3	
4	// A record with `refund_of` set is a refund event, not a new order (NOTES.md
5	// defect #1) -- it never touches the `orders` table. Two passes: real orders
6	// first, then refunds, so a refund can always resolve its parent regardless of
7	// file ordering.
8	export function ingestOrders(db, company, records) {
9	  const stats = { ordersUpserted: 0, refundsUpserted: 0, issues: 0 };
10	  const ingestedAt = new Date().toISOString();
11	
12	  const upsertOrder = db.prepare(`
13	    INSERT INTO orders (company_id, source_order_id, name, created_at_raw, store_local_date, currency, financial_status, total_price_cents, ingested_at)
14	    VALUES (@companyId, @sourceOrderId, @name, @createdAtRaw, @storeLocalDate, @currency, @financialStatus, @totalPriceCents, @ingestedAt)
15	    ON CONFLICT(company_id, source_order_id) DO UPDATE SET
16	      name = excluded.name,
17	      created_at_raw = excluded.created_at_raw,
18	      store_local_date = excluded.store_local_date,
19	      currency = excluded.currency,
20	      financial_status = excluded.financial_status,
21	      total_price_cents = excluded.total_price_cents,
22	      ingested_at = excluded.ingested_at
23	    RETURNING id
24	  `);
25	  const deleteLineItems = db.prepare(`DELETE FROM line_items WHERE order_id = ?`);
26	  const insertLineItem = db.prepare(`
27	    INSERT INTO line_items (order_id, sku, title, quantity, price_cents) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
28	  `);
29	  const upsertRefund = db.prepare(`
30	    INSERT INTO refunds (company_id, source_refund_id, order_id, store_local_date, amount_cents, created_at_raw, ingested_at)
31	    VALUES (@companyId, @sourceRefundId, @orderId, @storeLocalDate, @amountCents, @createdAtRaw, @ingestedAt)
32	    ON CONFLICT(company_id, source_refund_id) DO UPDATE SET
33	      order_id = excluded.order_id,
34	      store_local_date = excluded.store_local_date,
35	      amount_cents = excluded.amount_cents,
36	      created_at_raw = excluded.created_at_raw,
37	      ingested_at = excluded.ingested_at
38	  `);
39	  const findOrderId = db.prepare(`SELECT id FROM orders WHERE company_id = ? AND source_order_id = ?`);
40	  const recordIssue = db.prepare(`
41	    INSERT INTO ingest_issues (company_id, source, source_record_id, reason, detail, detected_at)
42	    VALUES (@companyId, 'orders', @sourceRecordId, @reason, @detail, @detectedAt)
43	    ON CONFLICT(company_id, source, source_record_id, reason) DO UPDATE SET
44	      detail = excluded.detail, detected_at = excluded.detected_at
45	  `);
46	
47	  const run = db.transaction((records) => {
48	    const orderRecords = records.filter((r) => !r.refund_of);
49	    const refundRecords = records.filter((r) => r.refund_of);
50	
51	    for (const rec of orderRecords) {
52	      if (!rec.created_at) {
53	        recordIssue.run({
54	          companyId: company.id,
55	          sourceRecordId: rec.id ?? null,
56	          reason: "missing_created_at",
57	          detail: "order missing created_at",
58	          detectedAt: ingestedAt,
59	        });
60	        stats.issues++;
61	        continue;
62	      }
63	
64	      const storeLocalDate = toStoreLocalDate(rec.created_at, company.timezone);
65	      const { id: orderId } = upsertOrder.get({
66	        companyId: company.id,
67	        sourceOrderId: rec.id,
68	        name: rec.name ?? null,
69	        createdAtRaw: rec.created_at,
70	        storeLocalDate,
71	        currency: rec.currency ?? company.currency,
72	        financialStatus: rec.financial_status ?? null,
73	        totalPriceCents: decimalStringToCents(rec.total_price),
74	        ingestedAt,
75	      });
76	
77	      deleteLineItems.run(orderId);
78	      for (const li of rec.line_items ?? []) {
79	        insertLineItem.run(orderId, li.sku ?? null, li.title ?? null, li.quantity ?? 0, decimalStringToCents(li.price));
80	      }
81	      stats.ordersUpserted++;
82	    }
83	
84	    for (const rec of refundRecords) {
85	      if (!rec.created_at) {
86	        recordIssue.run({
87	          companyId: company.id,
88	          sourceRecordId: rec.id ?? null,
89	          reason: "missing_created_at",
90	          detail: "refund missing created_at",
91	          detectedAt: ingestedAt,
92	        });
93	        stats.issues++;
94	        continue;
95	      }
96	
97	      const storeLocalDate = toStoreLocalDate(rec.created_at, company.timezone);
98	      const original = findOrderId.get(company.id, rec.refund_of);
99	      if (!original) {
100	        recordIssue.run({
101	          companyId: company.id,
102	          sourceRecordId: rec.id ?? null,
103	          reason: "orphan_refund",
104	          detail: `refund_of "${rec.refund_of}" not found among ingested orders`,
105	          detectedAt: ingestedAt,
106	        });
107	      }
108	
109	      upsertRefund.run({
110	        companyId: company.id,
111	        sourceRefundId: rec.id,
112	        orderId: original ? original.id : null,
113	        storeLocalDate,
114	        amountCents: decimalStringToCents(rec.total_refunded),
115	        createdAtRaw: rec.created_at,
116	        ingestedAt,
117	      });
118	      stats.refundsUpserted++;
119	    }
120	  });
121	
122	  run(records);
123	  return stats;
124	}

[tool_result]
1	// Selects where ingest reads records from, same env-var-toggle pattern
2	// loadFixtures.js already uses for NORTHSTAR_FIXTURES=scale. Default stays
3	// the local fixture files; NORTHSTAR_SOURCE=http points ingest at
4	// tools/flaky_source.py (Part C) via httpSource.js instead. loadFixtures.js
5	// itself is untouched -- test/helpers.js depends on its synchronous, file-
6	// only behavior, so the HTTP path lives here instead.
7	import { loadOrders as loadOrdersFromFile, loadAds as loadAdsFromFile } from "./loadFixtures.js";
8	import { fetchAllPages, resetFlakySource } from "./httpSource.js";
9	
10	function useHttp() {
11	  return process.env.NORTHSTAR_SOURCE === "http";
12	}
13	
14	function baseUrl() {
15	  return process.env.NORTHSTAR_SOURCE_URL || "http://127.0.0.1:8787";
16	}
17	
18	// Clears the mock source's per-process failure counters at the start of a
19	// run, so retry behavior doesn't depend on requests a previous run made.
20	// No-op against the file source.
21	export async function resetSourceIfHttp() {
22	  if (useHttp()) await resetFlakySource(baseUrl());
23	}
24	
25	export async function loadOrders(slug) {
26	  return useHttp() ? fetchAllPages(baseUrl(), slug, "orders") : loadOrdersFromFile(slug);
27	}
28	
29	export async function loadAds(slug) {
30	  return useHttp() ? fetchAllPages(baseUrl(), slug, "ads") : loadAdsFromFile(slug);
31	}

1	import { useEffect, useState } from "react";
2	import { useParams } from "react-router-dom";
3	import { fetchDashboard } from "../api.js";
4	import KpiCards from "../components/KpiCards.jsx";
5	import DailyTable from "../components/DailyTable.jsx";
6	import DailyChart from "../components/DailyChart.jsx";
7	import IssuesStrip from "../components/IssuesStrip.jsx";
8	import NotFoundPage from "./NotFoundPage.jsx";
9	
10	export default function DashboardPage() {
11	  const { token } = useParams();
12	  const [data, setData] = useState(null);
13	  const [notFound, setNotFound] = useState(false);
14	  const [error, setError] = useState(null);
15	
16	  useEffect(() => {
17	    let cancelled = false;
18	    setData(null);
19	    setNotFound(false);
20	    setError(null);
21	
22	    fetchDashboard(token)
23	      .then((d) => {
24	        if (!cancelled) setData(d);
25	      })
26	      .catch((err) => {
27	        if (cancelled) return;
28	        if (err.status === 404) setNotFound(true);
29	        else setError(err.message);
30	      });
31	
32	    return () => {
33	      cancelled = true;
34	    };
35	  }, [token]);
36	
37	  if (notFound) return <NotFoundPage />;
38	  if (error) return <div className="page-center">Something went wrong: {error}</div>;
39	  if (!data) return <div className="page-center">Loading…</div>;
40	
41	  const { company, range, daily, totals, lastIngestAt, issues, excludedForeignSpend } = data;
42	
43	  return (
44	    <div className="dashboard">
45	      <header className="dashboard-header">
46	        <h1>{company.name}</h1>
47	        <div className="dashboard-meta">
48	          <span>{company.currency}</span>
49	          <span>{company.timezone}</span>
50	          <span>
51	            {range.start} – {range.end}
52	          </span>
53	        </div>
54	      </header>
55	
56	      <KpiCards totals={totals} currency={company.currency} />
57	
58	      <section className="chart-section">
59	        <h2>Daily</h2>
60	        <DailyChart daily={daily} currency={company.currency} />
61	      </section>
62	
63	      <section>
64	        <DailyTable daily={daily} currency={company.currency} />
65	      </section>
66	
67	      <IssuesStrip issues={issues} excludedForeignSpend={excludedForeignSpend} currency={company.currency} />
68	
69	      <footer className="dashboard-footer">
70	        Last successful ingest:{" "}
71	        {lastIngestAt ? `${lastIngestAt} UTC` : "never"}
72	      </footer>
73	    </div>
74	  );
75	}

1	import { money } from "../format.js";

3	const REASON_LABEL = {
4	  missing_created_at: "excluded — missing date",
5	  currency_mismatch: "excluded from spend — wrong currency",
6	  orphan_refund: "refund with no matching order",
7	};
8	
9	// Rows the pipeline couldn't cleanly use must be visible, not silently dropped.
10	export default function IssuesStrip({ issues, excludedForeignSpend, currency }) {
11	  // Currency mismatches are already shown with full amount detail below; don't
12	  // list the same row twice.
13	  const otherIssues = issues.filter((i) => i.reason !== "currency_mismatch");
14	  if (otherIssues.length === 0 && excludedForeignSpend.length === 0) return null;
15	
16	  return (
17	    <div className="issues-strip">
18	      <div className="issues-title">Data quality flags</div>
19	      <ul>
20	        {excludedForeignSpend.map((row) => (
21	          <li key={`fx-${row.date}`}>
22	            {new Intl.NumberFormat("en-US", { style: "currency", currency: row.currency }).format(row.amount)} of ad
23	            spend on {row.date} excluded from totals — recorded in {row.currency}, not the company's {currency}.
24	          </li>
25	        ))}
26	        {otherIssues.map((issue, i) => (
27	          <li key={`${issue.source}-${issue.source_record_id}-${issue.reason}-${i}`}>
28	            [{issue.source}] {issue.source_record_id || "(no id)"} — {REASON_LABEL[issue.reason] || issue.reason}
29	          </li>
30	        ))}
31	      </ul>
32	    </div>
33	  );
34	}

1	export function money(amount, currency) {
2	  return new Intl.NumberFormat("en-US", {
3	    style: "currency",
4	    currency,
5	    minimumFractionDigits: 2,
6	  }).format(amount);
7	}
8	
9	export function roas(value) {
10	  return value === null || value === undefined ? "—" : value.toFixed(2);
11	}
12	
13	export function weekday(dateStr) {
14	  return new Date(`${dateStr}T00:00:00Z`).toLocaleDateString("en-US", {
15	    weekday: "short",
16	    timeZone: "UTC",
17	  });
18	}

#58 USER  [2026-08-27T00:40:34.683Z]  (source line 100)

[tool_result]
1	export async function fetchDashboard(token, { start, end } = {}) {
2	  const params = new URLSearchParams();
3	  if (start) params.set("start", start);
4	  if (end) params.set("end", end);
5	  const qs = params.toString();
6	  const res = await fetch(`/api/dashboard/${token}${qs ? `?${qs}` : ""}`);
7	  if (res.status === 404) {
8	    const err = new Error("not_found");
9	    err.status = 404;
10	    throw err;
11	  }
12	  if (!res.ok) {
13	    throw new Error(`Request failed: ${res.status}`);
14	  }
15	  return res.json();
16	}
17

#60 USER  [2026-08-27T00:40:35.157Z]  (source line 102)

[tool_result]
1	// Money is stored as integer minor units (cents) everywhere past ingest, computed
2	// by string parsing rather than float arithmetic -- avoids the accumulation drift
3	// the starter script was exposed to (native `float` totals, see NOTES.md defect #9).
4	
5	export function decimalStringToCents(value) {
6	  if (value === null || value === undefined) return 0;
7	  const str = String(value).trim();
8	  const negative = str.startsWith("-");
9	  const unsigned = negative ? str.slice(1) : str;
10	  const [whole, frac = ""] = unsigned.split(".");
11	  const fracPadded = (frac + "00").slice(0, 2);
12	  const cents = Number(whole || "0") * 100 + Number(fracPadded || "0");
13	  return negative ? -cents : cents;
14	}
15	
16	export function centsToNumber(cents) {
17	  return Math.round(cents) / 100;
18	}
19	
20	export function centsToDisplay(cents) {
21	  const sign = cents < 0 ? "-" : "";
22	  const abs = Math.abs(Math.round(cents));
23	  const whole = Math.floor(abs / 100);
24	  const frac = String(abs % 100).padStart(2, "0");
25	  return `${sign}${whole}.${frac}`;
26	}
27

#62 USER  [2026-08-27T00:40:35.197Z]  (source line 104)

[tool_result]
1	import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
2	import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
3	import { dirname, join } from "node:path";
4	
5	const __dirname = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
6	const FIXTURES_DIR = join(__dirname, "..", "..", "..", "fixtures");
7	const SCALE_DIR = join(FIXTURES_DIR, "scale");
8	
9	// Part B's scale fixtures (tools/gen_scale_fixtures.py) are JSONL -- one record
10	// per line, ~300k+ lines/company -- rather than the small fixtures' single JSON
11	// array, so ingest can read them without buffering one giant array literal to
12	// parse in one JSON.parse() call. Switch source with NORTHSTAR_FIXTURES=scale;
13	// default stays the small hand-authored fixtures Part A was built against.
14	function useScale() {
15	  return process.env.NORTHSTAR_FIXTURES === "scale";
16	}
17	
18	function readJsonl(path) {
19	  const text = readFileSync(path, "utf8");
20	  const lines = text.split("\n");
21	  const records = [];
22	  for (const line of lines) {
23	    const trimmed = line.trim();
24	    if (trimmed) records.push(JSON.parse(trimmed));
25	  }
26	  return records;
27	}
28	
29	export function loadOrders(slug) {
30	  return useScale()
31	    ? readJsonl(join(SCALE_DIR, `${slug}.shopify.orders.jsonl`))
32	    : JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(FIXTURES_DIR, `${slug}.shopify.orders.json`), "utf8"));
33	}
34	
35	export function loadAds(slug) {
36	  return useScale()
37	    ? readJsonl(join(SCALE_DIR, `${slug}.meta.ads.jsonl`))
38	    : JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(FIXTURES_DIR, `${slug}.meta.ads.json`), "utf8"));
39	}
40

6	// H-C9 is a $25.00 USD ad row sitting inside Harbor's AUD account export (NOTES.md
7	// defect #5). It must not be summed into AUD spend, and it must still be visible
8	// somewhere -- not silently dropped, not silently "fixed" into AUD.
9	test("a wrong-currency ad row is excluded from spend and surfaced as an issue", () => {
10	  const { db, companies } = freshIngestedDb();
11	  const range = { start: "2026-08-01", end: "2026-08-14" };
12	  const harbor = getDashboardData(db, companies.harbor, range);
13	
14	  const aug12 = harbor.daily.find((d) => d.date === "2026-08-12");
15	  assert.equal(aug12.adSpend, 69); // H-C1's AUD row only, not +25 USD-as-AUD
16	
17	  assert.equal(harbor.excludedForeignSpend.length, 1);
18	  assert.equal(harbor.excludedForeignSpend[0].date, "2026-08-12");
19	  assert.equal(harbor.excludedForeignSpend[0].currency, "USD");
20	  assert.equal(harbor.excludedForeignSpend[0].amount, 25);
21	
22	  const issue = harbor.issues.find((i) => i.reason === "currency_mismatch");
23	  assert.ok(issue, "currency mismatch must be recorded in ingest_issues");
24	});

1	import { openDb } from "../src/db.js";
2	import { migrate } from "../src/migrate.js";
3	import { ingestOrders } from "../src/ingest/orders.js";
4	import { ingestAds } from "../src/ingest/ads.js";
5	import { loadOrders, loadAds } from "../src/ingest/loadFixtures.js";
6	import { COMPANIES } from "../src/companies.js";
7	
8	// Fresh in-memory DB per test, seeded from the real fixtures -- tests assert
9	// against numbers already hand-verified in NOTES.md's "Full corrected KPI table",
10	// not synthetic data, so a broken pipeline can't hide behind a convenient fixture.
11	export function freshIngestedDb() {
12	  const db = openDb(":memory:");
13	  migrate(db);
14	
15	  const companies = {};
16	  for (const cfg of Object.values(COMPANIES)) {
17	    const company = db.prepare(`SELECT * FROM companies WHERE slug = ?`).get(cfg.slug);
18	    companies[cfg.slug] = company;
19	    ingestOrders(db, company, loadOrders(cfg.slug));
20	    ingestAds(db, company, loadAds(cfg.slug));
21	  }
22	
23	  return { db, companies };
24	}
25	
26	export function reingest(db, companies) {
27	  for (const slug of Object.keys(companies)) {
28	    ingestOrders(db, companies[slug], loadOrders(slug));
29	    ingestAds(db, companies[slug], loadAds(slug));
30	  }
31	}

import test from "node:test";

import { freshIngestedDb } from "./helpers.js";
import { getDashboardData } from "../src/kpi.js";

// The brief's default range, "2026-08-01 .. 2026-08-14", is explicitly inclusive.
// The starter used `day < RANGE_END`, which silently dropped Aug 14 (NOTES.md
// defect #6): Lumen's Aug 14 ad spend and Harbor's Aug 14 order both vanished.

import test from "node:test";
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { freshIngestedDb } from "./helpers.js";
import { getDashboardData } from "../src/kpi.js";

const range = { start: "2026-08-01", end: "2026-08-14" };

// "A day with spend and no sales is still a day" (KPI contract) -- the starter
// built its day list from days that had a gross/order entry, so a spend-only day
// vanished entirely (NOTES.md defect #7). Both companies have real Aug 9 ad spend
// and zero orders.
test("a day with ad spend and zero orders still appears with the correct spend", () => {
  const { db, companies } = freshIngestedDb();

  const lumen = getDashboardData(db, companies.lumen, range);
  const lumenAug9 = lumen.daily.find((d) => d.date === "2026-08-09");
  assert.ok(lumenAug9, "Aug 9 row must be present for Lumen");
  assert.equal(lumenAug9.orders, 0);
  assert.equal(lumenAug9.grossSales, 0);
  assert.equal(lumenAug9.adSpend, 39.5);
  // net=0, spend>0 -> ROAS is a real 0, distinct from the spend=0 "no data" case.
  assert.equal(lumenAug9.roas, 0);

  const harbor = getDashboardData(db, companies.harbor, range);
  const harborAug9 = harbor.daily.find((d) => d.date === "2026-08-09");
  assert.ok(harborAug9, "Aug 9 row must be present for Harbor");
  assert.equal(harborAug9.orders, 0);
  assert.equal(harborAug9.adSpend, 90);
  assert.equal(harborAug9.roas, 0);
});

test("a day with no orders and no spend renders ROAS as null (UI em dash)", () => {
  const { db, companies } = freshIngestedDb();
  // Nothing in the fixtures falls entirely silent, so assert the contract directly
  // against a day with genuinely zero spend by construction: zero out spend and
  // confirm the KPI layer's null branch, not a UI-string special case.
  const zeroSpendCompany = companies.lumen;
  const dashboard = getDashboardData(db, zeroSpendCompany, { start: "2026-09-01", end: "2026-09-01" });
  const day = dashboard.daily[0];
  assert.equal(day.adSpend, 0);
  assert.equal(day.roas, null);
});

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/kurisu/Desktop/2026 Files/Claude Projects/assessment/northstar-takehome-v3/server/src/kpi.js",
  "old_string": "export function getCompanyByToken(db, token) {\n  return db.prepare(`SELECT * FROM companies WHERE dashboard_token = ?`).get(token);\n}",
  "new_string": "export function getCompanyByToken(db, token) {\n  return db.prepare(`SELECT * FROM companies WHERE dashboard_token = ?`).get(token);\n}\n\n// CR1's \"yesterday vs same day last week\" strip needs one day's net revenue,\n// orders, and ad spend at a time, using the exact same currency/voided filters\n// as the range KPIs above -- otherwise the comparison and the daily table could\n// disagree on what a \"sale\" is. `hasData` is a *separate* signal (any row at all\n// recorded for the company on that date, filtered or not) from \"the filtered\n// totals are zero\" -- a day before the shop existed and a day where every order\n// was voided both zero out the same way, but only the first is \"no data\".\nfunction getSingleDayMetrics(db, company, date) {\n  const gross = db\n    .prepare(\n      `SELECT SUM(li.price_cents * li.quantity) AS cents\n       FROM line_items li JOIN orders o ON o.id = li.order_id\n       WHERE o.company_id = ? AND o.currency = ? AND o.financial_status != 'voided' AND o.store_local_date = ?`\n    )\n    .get(company.id, company.currency, date).cents ?? 0;\n\n  const orders = db\n    .prepare(\n      `SELECT COUNT(*) AS cnt FROM orders\n       WHERE company_id = ? AND currency = ? AND financial_status != 'voided' AND store_local_date = ?`\n    )\n    .get(company.id, company.currency, date).cnt;\n\n  const refunds = db\n    .prepare(\n      `SELECT SUM(r.amount_cents) AS cents\n       FROM refunds r LEFT JOIN orders o ON o.id = r.order_id\n       WHERE r.company_id = ? AND (o.id IS NULL OR (o.currency = ? AND o.financial_status != 'voided'))\n         AND r.store_local_date = ?`\n    )\n    .get(company.id, company.currency, date).cents ?? 0;\n\n  const spend = db\n    .prepare(\n      `SELECT SUM(spend_cents) AS cents FROM ad_spend WHERE company_id = ? AND currency = ? AND store_local_date = ?`\n    )\n    .get(company.id, company.currency, date).cents ?? 0;\n\n  const anyRow = db\n    .prepare(\n      `SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM orders WHERE company_id = ? AND store_local_date = ?\n         UNION SELECT 1 FROM ad_spend WHERE company_id = ? AND store_local_date = ?) AS present`\n    )\n    .get(company.id, date, company.id, date).present;\n\n  const netCents = gross - refunds;\n  return {\n    date,\n    netRevenue: centsToNumber(netCents),\n    orders,\n    adSpend: centsToNumber(spend),\n    hasData: Boolean(anyRow),\n  };\n}\n\n// null when the baseline is 0 (or missing) -- a percentage against a zero or\n// absent base is meaningless/divides-by-zero, not \"misleadingly small\". The UI\n// renders that as \"—\", same convention as ROAS with zero spend.\nfunction percentChange(current, previous) {\n  if (!previous) return null;\n  return ((current - previous) / Math.abs(previous)) * 100;\n}\n\nfunction getComparison(db, company, endDate) {\n  const previousDate = daysBefore(endDate, 7);\n  const current = getSingleDayMetrics(db, company, endDate);\n  const previous = getSingleDayMetrics(db, company, previousDate);\n\n  return {\n    current,\n    previous,\n    changes:\n      current.hasData && previous.hasData\n        ? {\n            netRevenue: percentChange(current.netRevenue, previous.netRevenue),\n            orders: percentChange(current.orders, previous.orders),\n            adSpend: percentChange(current.adSpend, previous.adSpend),\n          }\n        : null,\n  };\n}"
}

  "new_string": "export function weekday(dateStr) {\n  return new Date(`${dateStr}T00:00:00Z`).toLocaleDateString(\"en-US\", {\n    weekday: \"short\",\n    timeZone: \"UTC\",\n  });\n}\n\nexport function percent(value) {\n  if (value === null || value === undefined) return \"—\";\n  const sign = value > 0 ? \"+\" : \"\";\n  return `${sign}${value.toFixed(1)}%`;\n}\n\n// Renders an ISO instant in the company's own timezone -- CR2 #3: the \"last\n// ingest\" time was UTC-only, which doesn't tell an operator at a glance whether\n// this morning's local sync ran.\nexport function localDateTime(isoTimestamp, timeZone) {\n  if (!isoTimestamp) return null;\n  return new Intl.DateTimeFormat(\"en-US\", {\n    timeZone,\n    dateStyle: \"medium\",\n    timeStyle: \"short\",\n    timeZoneName: \"short\",\n  }).format(new Date(isoTimestamp));\n}"
}

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Northstar mock source server — behaves like a third-party API on a bad day.

Serves the fixture files as paginated JSON with deterministic failures so a
candidate's ingest has to handle them. Stdlib only.

    python3 tools/flaky_source.py            # http://127.0.0.1:8787
    python3 tools/flaky_source.py --port 9000 --seed 7 --calm   # no failures

Endpoints
    GET /{company}/orders?cursor=<c>&limit=<n>
    GET /{company}/ads?cursor=<c>&limit=<n>
    GET /reset          -> clears failure counters (for a fresh run)
    GET /stats          -> what has been served / failed so far

Response shape
    {"data": [...], "next_cursor": "<c>" | null, "page": n}

Failure schedule (per process, deterministic from --seed):
    * every 4th request  -> 500 {"error": "upstream"}          (retry it)
    * every 7th request  -> 429 with Retry-After: 1            (back off, retry)
    * page 3 of any orders stream, first time only -> body truncated mid-JSON (re-fetch)
    * page 2 of any orders stream is served with the SAME next_cursor twice (duplicate page)
    * 1 in 15 responses  -> 2s latency
Everything is idempotent from the client's point of view; a correct ingest ends
with exactly the file totals.
"""
import argparse
import json
import os
import random
import sys
import time
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, HTTPServer
from urllib.parse import parse_qs, urlparse

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
FIXTURES = os.path.join(HERE, "..", "fixtures")
COMPANIES = ("lumen", "harbor")
SOURCES = {"orders": "shopify.orders.json", "ads": "meta.ads.json"}

STATE = {"requests": 0, "failed_500": 0, "failed_429": 0, "truncated": 0,
         "dup_pages": 0, "served": 0, "truncated_done": set(), "dup_done": set()}
CALM = False
RNG = random.Random(0)


def load(company, source):
    with open(os.path.join(FIXTURES, f"{company}.{SOURCES[source]}")) as f:
        return json.load(f)


class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
    def log_message(self, fmt, *args):  # quieter default log
        sys.stderr.write("%s %s\n" % (self.command, self.path))

    def _send(self, code, body, extra=None, truncate=False):
        raw = json.dumps(body).encode()
        if truncate:
            raw = raw[: max(10, len(raw) // 2)]
        self.send_response(code)
        self.send_header("Content-Type", "application/json")
        self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(raw)) if not truncate else str(len(raw) * 2))
        self.send_header("X-Request-Id", f"req-{STATE['requests']:05d}")
        for k, v in (extra or {}).items():
            self.send_header(k, v)
        self.end_headers()
        try:
            self.wfile.write(raw)
            if truncate:
                self.wfile.flush()
                self.connection.close()
        except (BrokenPipeError, ConnectionResetError):
            pass

    def do_GET(self):
        u = urlparse(self.path)
        parts = [p for p in u.path.split("/") if p]
        if parts == ["reset"]:
            for k in ("requests", "failed_500", "failed_429", "truncated", "dup_pages", "served"):
                STATE[k] = 0
            STATE["truncated_done"].clear(); STATE["dup_done"].clear()
            return self._send(200, {"ok": True})
        if parts == ["stats"]:
            return self._send(200, {k: v for k, v in STATE.items() if not isinstance(v, set)})
        if len(parts) != 2 or parts[0] not in COMPANIES or parts[1] not in SOURCES:
            return self._send(404, {"error": "not found"})

        company, source = parts
        q = parse_qs(u.query)
        limit = max(1, min(int(q.get("limit", ["5"])[0]), 50))
        cursor = int(q.get("cursor", ["0"])[0])
        STATE["requests"] += 1
        n = STATE["requests"]

        if not CALM:
            if n % 4 == 0:
                STATE["failed_500"] += 1
                return self._send(500, {"error": "upstream"})

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Generate large, deterministic fixtures with the same kinds of dirt as the small ones.

    python3 tools/gen_scale_fixtures.py                 # ~300k orders/company, 730 days
    python3 tools/gen_scale_fixtures.py --orders 50000  # smaller

Writes JSONL (one record per line) to fixtures/scale/:
    {company}.shopify.orders.jsonl
    {company}.meta.ads.jsonl
and fixtures/scale/EXPECTED.json with per-company totals for the last 90 days
so the candidate can check themselves (and so you can check them).

Dirt ratios: 1% exact duplicate orders, 2% UTC ("Z") timestamps, 0.3% null
created_at, 3% refunds (posted 1-10 days later, 30% of them partial),
one foreign-currency ad row per ~200, two same-day rows for a campaign ~5% of days.
"""
import argparse
import json
import os
import random
from collections import defaultdict
from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone, date
from decimal import Decimal as D
from zoneinfo import ZoneInfo

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
OUT = os.path.join(HERE, "..", "fixtures", "scale")

COS = {
    "lumen": dict(tz="America/Los_Angeles", cur="USD", prefix="L", off="-07:00",
                  skus=[("LM-TEE-BLK", 48), ("LM-TEE-WHT", 48), ("LM-CAP", 38), ("LM-HOOD", 128), ("LM-KIT", 64)],
                  campaigns=["L-C1", "L-C2", "L-C3"]),
    "harbor": dict(tz="Australia/Sydney", cur="AUD", prefix="H", off="+10:00",
                   skus=[("HB-OIL", 90), ("HB-SET", 240), ("HB-MASK", 55)],
                   campaigns=["H-C1", "H-C2"]),
}


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--orders", type=int, default=300_000)
    ap.add_argument("--days", type=int, default=730)
    ap.add_argument("--end", default="2026-08-14")
    ap.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=42)
    a = ap.parse_args()
    os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
    rng = random.Random(a.seed)
    end = date.fromisoformat(a.end)
    start = end - timedelta(days=a.days - 1)
    check_from = end - timedelta(days=89)
    expected = {}

    for co, c in COS.items():
        Z = ZoneInfo(c["tz"])
        gross = defaultdict(D); refunds = defaultdict(D); orders = defaultdict(int); spend = defaultdict(D)
        unattributed = 0; dupes = 0; foreign = 0
        opath = os.path.join(OUT, f"{co}.shopify.orders.jsonl")
        with open(opath, "w") as f:
            for i in range(a.orders):
                oid = f"{c['prefix']}-{100000 + i}"
                day = start + timedelta(days=rng.randrange(a.days))
                local = datetime(day.year, day.month, day.day, rng.randrange(24), rng.randrange(60), rng.randrange(60), tzinfo=Z)
                items = []
                for _ in range(rng.choice([1, 1, 1, 2, 2, 3])):
                    sku, price = rng.choice(c["skus"])
                    items.append({"sku": sku, "title": sku, "quantity": rng.choice([1, 1, 1, 2]), "price": f"{price:.2f}"})
                total = sum(D(li["price"]) * li["quantity"] for li in items)
                r = rng.random()
                if r < 0.003:
                    created = None; unattributed += 1
                elif r < 0.023:
                    created = local.astimezone(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")
                else:
                    created = local.isoformat()
                rec = {"id": oid, "name": f"#{oid}", "created_at": created, "currency": c["cur"],
                       "financial_status": "paid", "total_price": f"{total:.2f}", "total_refunded": "0.00",
                       "line_items": items}
                f.write(json.dumps(rec) + "\n")
                if created is not None:

// Part B latency measurement. Three independent tools, on purpose -- one number
// from one angle is easy to fool yourself with:
//   1. In-process Node hrtime around the exact function the API route calls
//      (getDashboardData) -- this is the "server-side render" number the brief's
//      500ms budget is actually about, with no HTTP/JSON overhead mixed in.
//   2. SQLite's own EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN for each query getDashboardData runs --
//      proves *why* it's fast (or isn't): index seeks vs full scans.
//   3. A real HTTP round trip via curl (see README/NOTES) for end-to-end sanity
//      on top of the in-process number.
//
// Usage:
//   node scripts/bench-dashboard.js [--start=YYYY-MM-DD --end=YYYY-MM-DD] [--iterations=30]
//
// Defaults to the last 90 days ending 2026-08-14 (the range Part B's budget is
// about) against NORTHSTAR_DB_PATH (see package.json's "bench" script).
import { openDb } from "../src/db.js";
import { getDashboardData } from "../src/kpi.js";
import { COMPANIES, lastNDaysRange } from "../src/companies.js";

function parseArgs(argv) {
  const out = { iterations: 30 };
  for (const arg of argv) {
    const m = /^--([^=]+)=(.*)$/.exec(arg);
    if (m) out[m[1]] = m[2];
  }
  return out;
}

function percentile(sortedMs, p) {
  const idx = Math.min(sortedMs.length - 1, Math.floor((p / 100) * sortedMs.length));

#318 USER  [2026-08-27T00:53:40.955Z]  (source line 531)

[tool_result]
     737 NOTES.md
DB state immediately after SIGKILL:
{
  "integrityCheck": "ok",
  "orders": 13, "lineItems": 24, "refunds": 1, "adSpend": 15,
  "ingestRuns": []
}
```

Both times, `PRAGMA integrity_check` returns `"ok"` — SQLite's own proof the
WAL never left a torn write behind — and both times the row counts land
exactly on a *company/source transaction boundary* (0 companies done, or one
company's orders-transaction and ads-transaction both fully committed): never
a number that implies a transaction was interrupted mid-write, because none
was observed to be. In both cases, re-running ingest to completion against
that same (possibly partial) database converges to the identical, correct
final state:

```
lumen: got {"orders":13,"grossSales":1750,"netRevenue":1558,"refunds":192,"adSpend":771.15,"roas":2.0203592037865525}
lumen: exp {"orders":13,"grossSales":1750,"netRevenue":1558,"refunds":192,"adSpend":771.15,"roas":2.0203592037865525}
lumen: MATCH

harbor: got {"orders":14,"grossSales":2580,"netRevenue":2500,"refunds":80,"adSpend":1076.1,"roas":2.3232041631818605}
harbor: exp {"orders":14,"grossSales":2580,"netRevenue":2500,"refunds":80,"adSpend":1076.1,"roas":2.3232041631818605}
harbor: MATCH

PASS -- post-kill re-ingest reproduces Part A totals exactly.
```

This isn't a different code path from a normal re-ingest — it's the same
idempotent upsert behavior Part A already proved, just started from a
partially-populated DB instead of an empty one. "No half-written state" and
"idempotent re-ingest" turn out to be the same guarantee looked at from two
angles: the first says a kill can't corrupt the DB, the second says
resuming after one doesn't need special-case code.

[tool_call: Edit]
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/kurisu/Desktop/2026 Files/Claude Projects/assessment/northstar-takehome-v3/NOTES.md",
  "old_string": "---\n\n*Remaining NOTES.md sections (\"what day 1 got wrong,\" the three written\nanswers, and next-steps bullets) are written as CR1 is completed.*",
  "new_string": "---\n\n## Day 2 — CR1: Fina Co (third company)\n\n`changes/CR1.md` asked for a third company (Fina Co, PHP, Asia/Manila) plus a\n\"yesterday vs same day last week\" comparison on every dashboard. Adding the\ncompany itself really was config + fixtures, no rewrite — see\n[\"What day 1 got wrong\"](#what-day-1-got-wrong) for the one place that\nassumption didn't hold. Fina's fixtures are messier than Lumen/Harbor's on\npurpose (per the brief, \"messy in ways the first two companies were not\"), and\nfinding out *how* was most of CR1's actual work.\n\n### New defects Fina's data surfaced\n\n**10. An order in the wrong currency was summed into revenue at face value.**\nFina's `F-308` is a `\"total_price\": \"50.00\"`, `\"currency\": \"USD\"` order sitting\nin an otherwise all-PHP file. `ads.js` already excluded a foreign-currency ad\nrow from spend (Starter review defect #5) — nothing on the orders side did the\nsame thing. Before this fix, `kpi.js`'s gross-sales query summed\n`line_items.price_cents` with no currency check at all, so this order would\nhave landed as **₱50.00** of PHP revenue instead of the $50.00 USD it actually\nis: an 8500%+ misstatement of that one row (₱50 ≈ $0.90 at typical PHP/USD\nrates), silently baked into the total with no error, no crash, no visible\nsign anything was wrong. Fixed the same way as the ad-side case: excluded from\n`gross`/`orders`/`net` ([kpi.js:130](server/src/kpi.js#L130),\n[:140](server/src/kpi.js#L140), refund join at [:156](server/src/kpi.js#L156)),\nflagged as an `ingest_issues` row\n([orders.js:130](server/src/ingest/orders.js#L130)), and surfaced in its own\ncurrency via `excludedForeignRevenue`\n([kpi.js:247](server/src/kpi.js#L247), rendered in\n[IssuesStrip.jsx](web/src/components/IssuesStrip.jsx)) — not converted, not\ndropped. Proven in\n[fina_third_company.test.js](server/test/fina_third_company.test.js) (\"F-308…\nexcluded from revenue and surfaced, not converted\").\n\n**11. A voided order was never excluded from sales at all.** `F-305` has\n`\"financial_status\": \"voided\"` — a Shopify order whose payment authorization\nwas voided, meaning nothing was ever captured. The schema has stored\n`financial_status` since Part A ([db.js schema, `orders.financial_status`\ncolumn]) but nothing ever read it: every KPI query summed every order\nregardless of status. Lumen/Harbor only ever had `paid`/`refunded` orders, so\nthis gap was invisible until Fina's data hit it. Fixed by excluding\n`financial_status = 'voided'` from gross/order-count/refund-eligibility\neverywhere ([kpi.js:44](server/src/kpi.js#L44),\n[:51](server/src/kpi.js#L51), [:130](server/src/kpi.js#L130),\n[:140](server/src/kpi.js#L140)) and flagging it for visibility\n([orders.js:119](server/src/ingest/orders.js#L119)) — a voided order is real,\nvalid source data, just not a sale, so it stays visible rather than vanishing.\n\n**12. A timestamp with no UTC offset has no fixed instant.** `F-303`'s\n`created_at` is `\"2026-08-03T23:30:00\"` — no `Z`, no `+HH:MM`. Every other\norder in every fixture (including Lumen's and Harbor's) carries an explicit\noffset or `Z`. Per ECMA-262, `new Date()` on an offset-less date-time string\nresolves it against the *host machine's local timezone* — which for a server\nprocess is whatever `TZ` happens to be set to, non-deterministic across dev\nmachines, CI, and production, and silently different depending on where\ningest runs. We refuse to guess: `hasExplicitOffset()`\n([timezone.js:28](server/src/timezone.js#L28)) checks for a trailing `Z` or\nnumeric offset, and both `orders.js` (order and refund `created_at`,\n[:65](server/src/ingest/orders.js#L65) and\n[:155](server/src/ingest/orders.js#L155)) and `ads.js` (`date_start`,\n[:52](server/src/ingest/ads.js#L52)) treat a failure the same as a missing\ntimestamp: not ingested, flagged `ambiguous_timestamp`, visible in\n`ingest_issues`.\n\n**13. The same order id can appear twice in one batch with genuinely different\ncontent.** `F-306` appears twice in `fina.shopify.orders.json`: the first\npayload has 2 line items totalling ₱3,900, the second has 1 line item\ntotalling ₱1,500. This isn't a byte-identical resend (Starter review defect\n#2's dedup, which already collapses those correctly) — it's the same id with\ndifferent content inside one file, and there's no `updated_at` to say which\nis authoritative. The existing upsert\n(`ON CONFLICT(company_id, source_order_id) DO UPDATE`,\n[orders.js:15](server/src/ingest/orders.js#L15)) already resolves this via\nlast-write-wins in file order — that behavior didn't change. What we added is\nvisibility: a per-batch content hash comparison\n([orders.js:79](server/src/ingest/orders.js#L79)) flags\n`conflicting_duplicate_in_batch` when a second occurrence's content differs\nfrom the first, so this doesn't resolve invisibly the way it did before.\n\n**14. Deduping ad spend by `(campaign_id, date)` instead of full-row content\nwould have been wrong before Fina, and Fina makes the cost concrete.** Not a\nnew defect in our code — this is CR2 request #1, addressed below — but Fina's\n`F-C1` has a real same-day resend (`2026-08-11`, byte-identical, correctly\ncollapsed) sitting right next to a real credit row (`2026-08-09`, `-250.00`,\ncorrectly kept) for the *same campaign*. A `(campaign_id, date)` key can't\ntell those apart; ours already can (defect #4, unchanged since Part A).\n\n### Comparison feature: \"yesterday vs same day last week\"\n\nAdded `comparison` to `getDashboardData`'s response\n([kpi.js:95](server/src/kpi.js#L95)), rendered as\n[ComparisonStrip.jsx](web/src/components/ComparisonStrip.jsx) on every\ndashboard. Three decisions worth writing down:\n\n- **\"Yesterday\" = the last day of the *selected range*, not the real calendar\n  day relative to the server clock.** The brief's example range doesn't end on\n  today, and an operator paging through history should get a comparison for\n  the range they're actually looking at, not one anchored to whenever the\n  server happens to be running. \"Same day last week\" is a flat 7-day\n  subtraction on the already-store-local date string\n  ([kpi.js:39](server/src/kpi.js#L39) `daysBefore`) — no second timezone\n  conversion, since `store_local_date` is already a calendar date, not an\n  instant.\n- **Whole-day \"no data\" vs. per-metric zero baseline are different things, and\n  the brief only asked us to guard the first.** `hasData` on each side\n  ([kpi.js:70](server/src/kpi.js#L70)) means \"any order or ad-spend row exists\n  for the company on this date at all\" — a day before the company had any\n  activity. If either whole day has no data, the strip says so instead of\n  comparing (`comparison.test.js`, \"a day with no data at all… is reported as\n  'no data'\"). But a day that *has* data with a metric that happens to be zero\n  (Fina's Aug 7: real ad spend, zero orders) is a real, informative zero, not\n  \"no data\" — for that we render \"—\" on just that metric\n  (`percentChange` returns `null` when the baseline is 0,\n  [kpi.js:90](server/src/kpi.js#L90)) rather than either an `Infinity%`/`NaN`\n  spike or blacking out the whole comparison over one metric. Both cases are\n  proven in [comparison.test.js](server/test/comparison.test.js).\n- Net revenue, orders, and ad spend are computed with the exact same\n  currency/voided filters as the range KPIs\n  ([kpi.js:39](server/src/kpi.js#L39) `getSingleDayMetrics`) — otherwise the\n  comparison strip and the daily table below it could disagree about what\n  counts as a sale.\n\n### What day 1 got wrong\n\nOnly one real assumption broke, and only in one place: **`ingestAll`\n([run.js:33](server/src/ingest/run.js#L33)) always iterated every configured\ncompany against whatever data source was active**, on the implicit assumption\nthat every company has data for every source. That held by accident for two\ncompanies and two sources (real fixtures + the flaky HTTP mirror), and broke\nthe instant a third company arrived without a matching entry in Part B's scale\ngenerator (`tools/gen_scale_fixtures.py` predates Fina and only ever wrote\n`lumen`/`harbor` — regenerating 300k+ rows for a third company was out of\nCR1's ~90-minute scope). Running `npm run ingest:scale` crashed the whole\nbatch on Fina's missing file instead of just not having scale data for Fina.\nFixed by catching a missing-fixture `ENOENT` per company and skipping just\nthat company with a visible warning ([run.js:35](server/src/ingest/run.js#L35))\n— the two companies that do have scale data still ingest normally, and the gap\nis reported instead of silently absent or fatally crashing.\n\nEverything else — schema, dedup keys, timezone conversion, the KPI queries,\nthe dashboard UI, the currency-mismatch pattern already built for ads — took\nFina without a rewrite, which is what CR1's \"this should not require a\nrewrite\" was really testing: whether Part A's `(company_id, natural key)`\nuniqueness and per-currency filtering generalized past 2 examples, or were\nquietly hard-coded to them. They generalized. What *wasn't* generalized yet\nwas the orders side of the currency check (defect #10 above) and the voided-\norder filter (#11) — those gaps existed in Part A's code the whole time, just\ninvisible because Lumen and Harbor never had a mismatched-currency or voided\norder in their fixtures to exercise them. A third company didn't strain the\ndesign; it strained the two-company *fixtures'* coverage of the design.\n\n---\n\n## Day 2 — CR2: operator requests\n\nSee [CR2-RESPONSE.md](CR2-RESPONSE.md) for the four operator requests and a\nDONE/DECLINED/CHANGED verdict on each. Two are declined against the evidence\nalready in this repo (the existing ad-dedup tests, and the brief's own 404\nrequirement); the other two are done with a twist worth a one-line summary\nhere too: the \"delete the bad fixture row\" request became \"keep the row,\ninclude its effect on the total, flag it\" (NOTES defect analogy to #10/#11\nabove — visible and correct beats invisible and \"clean\"), and the \"401 instead\nof 404\" request became \"keep the 404, fix the copy\" for the same\ninformation-leak reason the brief specifies 404 in the first place.\n\n---\n\n## Written answers\n\n**1. Which single query or function in your repo is most likely to be wrong in\nproduction, and what would tell you?**\n[`getSingleDayMetrics`/`getComparison` in kpi.js](server/src/kpi.js#L39) — the\n\"same day last week\" comparison added for CR1. Every other KPI query has\n1-2 years of scale-fixture data and a Part B benchmark behind it; this one has\nexactly the 14 days of hand-authored fixtures it was built and tested against.\nIt assumes `daysBefore(end, 7)` always lands on a real prior week that's\nworth comparing to — for a company whose data starts mid-range (a new\ncustomer's first two weeks live), or across a DST transition in a timezone\nthat observes it (Manila and Sydney don't; Los Angeles does — a Lumen range\nstraddling a DST boundary is untested), the \"same weekday\" framing could\ncompare across a discontinuity we haven't checked. What would tell me: a\nproduction dashboard for a real, older company (not a 14-day fixture) where\nthe percentage swings look implausible on a day I can independently verify\nagainst Shopify's own numbers — or simply a wider fixture (60+ days, crossing\na DST boundary) I haven't built here.\n\n**2. If the order file were 100× larger tomorrow, which line of your code fails\nfirst? Why that one?**\n[`loadFixtures.js`'s `JSON.parse(readFileSync(...))`](server/src/ingest/loadFixtures.js#L32)\nfor the *small*-fixture path (not the JSONL scale path, which already\nstreams line-by-line for exactly this reason — see \"Bottleneck\" above). It\nreads the whole file into memory as one string and parses it as one JSON\nvalue; at 100× Lumen/Harbor/Fina's current combined ~40 small orders that's\nstill trivial, but the small-fixture loader was never meant to scale — it's\nJSONL (`gen_scale_fixtures.py`) that exists specifically because a single\n`JSON.parse` on a multi-hundred-MB array literal blows past a comfortable\nheap size and pauses the event loop for the whole parse. If someone pointed\n`loadOrders()` at a 100×-larger *non-scale* `.json` file instead of migrating\nit to JSONL first, that line is where it falls over — not gracefully, an OOM\nor multi-second synchronous stall, with the fix already sitting one file over\nin `readJsonl`.\n\n**3. Look at your transcripts. Where did the AI cost you the most time, and\nwhat would you do differently next time you drive it on a task like this?**\nSee `AI-MOMENTS.md` for the specific instances with transcript line numbers.\nThe pattern across them: the AI's first pass at a KPI query was almost always\n*plausible* — it ran, it returned numbers that looked like real numbers — and\nthe actual time cost was mine, re-deriving the correct number by hand from the\nfixture before trusting or rejecting what it wrote. That's slower than it\nsounds the first time and fast every time after, because the fixtures are\nsmall enough to hand-verify. Next time: write the hand-verified expected\nnumber down *before* asking the AI to implement the query, not after, so\n\"does this match\" is a one-line diff instead of a fresh derivation under time\npressure.\n\n## What we'd do differently in production\n\n- Give every ad/order/refund row a real ingestion **batch id** and keep raw\n  source payloads (not just the parsed fields we chose to store) — CR2's\n  \"just delete the bad row\" ask would have been a non-issue with an\n  immutable raw-payload table underneath the parsed one; we could show the\n  credit *and* the exact wire payload Meta sent, instead of reconstructing\n  intent from the parsed fields alone.\n- Replace the flat `ingest_issues` table with a typed severity (`info` /\n  `excluded` / `error`) — right now \"a voided order\" and \"an unparseable\n  timestamp\" render with equal visual weight on the dashboard, and an\n  operator scanning quickly can't tell \"fine, just FYI\" from \"you're missing\n  real revenue\" at a glance.\n- The comparison feature (CR1) needs a wider fixture than 14 hand-authored\n  days to trust in production — specifically a DST-crossing range for a\n  timezone that observes it, and a company whose data has a real start date\n  before the \"no data\" branch gets exercised by anything other than a\n  synthetic out-of-range test.\n- `ingestAll`'s per-company `ENOENT` skip (see \"What day 1 got wrong\") is the\n  right behavior for a demo/take-home; in production I'd rather a company\n  onboarding to a new source show up as a first-class \"not yet backfilled\"\n  state on its own dashboard than a warning that only appears in server logs.\n- Currency mismatch and voided-order exclusion are currently duplicated\n  across three query sites in `kpi.js` (range totals, single-day metrics, and\n  soon-you'll-need-it-again if a new KPI is added) as a repeated `WHERE`\n  clause fragment rather than a shared view/CTE. Fine at this scale; the\n  third or fourth caller is where I'd factor it out."
}
