  "content": "# CR2 Response\n\n`changes/CR2.md` — four requests from an operator call. Verdicts below; full\nreasoning and the code changes are in [NOTES.md](NOTES.md#day-2--cr2-operator-requests).\nI'm the engineer on this, so where I think an operator's literal ask would make\nthe product worse or contradict something the brief itself already specifies,\nI say so with evidence rather than just doing what was asked.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Duplicate ad rows — \"dedupe on campaign ID plus date\"\n\n**DECLINED.**\n\nWe already dedupe ad spend, but on a hash of the full row content\n([ads.js:9](server/src/ingest/ads.js#L9)), not `(campaign_id, date)`. That's\nnot an oversight — it's a deliberate fix from Part A (NOTES.md Starter review\ndefect #4): the starter's original `(campaign_id, date)` key treated \"same\ncampaign, same day\" as one event and kept whichever copy arrived last,\nwhich is correct for a byte-identical resend but silently **drops a second,\ngenuinely different spend event** for that campaign on that day.\n\nEvidence this isn't hypothetical:\n- [server/test/ad_dedup.test.js:42](server/test/ad_dedup.test.js#L42) — \"two\n  genuinely different spend events for the same campaign/day both count\":\n  two rows, same `campaign_id` (`X-2`), same `date` (`2026-08-01`), spends\n  `$10.00` and `$12.50`. A `(campaign_id, date)` key collapses these to one\n  row and drops $10 or $12.50 depending on which arrives last; ours keeps\n  both because the row content (including `date_start`, `spend`, timestamps)\n  differs.\n- Fina Co's real fixture data (CR1) has both cases sitting next to each other\n  in the same campaign: `F-C1` on `2026-08-11` is a genuine byte-identical\n  resend (both rows identical — content-hash dedup correctly keeps one), and\n  `F-C1` on `2026-08-09` has a real spend row *and* a real `-250.00` credit\n  row on the same date (content-hash dedup correctly keeps both, since their\n  content differs). A `(campaign_id, date)` key can't tell these two\n  situations apart — it would either drop the real second Aug-9 row or double\n  Aug-11's total, depending on tie-break order.\n\nDoing what was literally asked would reintroduce a bug we already found and\nfixed, and lose real spend data the moment Meta ever sends two distinct spend\nevents for one campaign on one day. What we have already achieves the\noperator's actual goal (a resend can't double-count spend) without that cost.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Wrong links — \"return 401 instead of a blank 404\"\n\n**CHANGED.**\n\nKept the HTTP status at 404. `CANDIDATE-BRIEF.md` (line 37) is explicit:\n*\"Wrong or unknown token → 404 (not 401, not a redirect).\"* This isn't\narbitrary — a private, unguessable link is the *only* access control this\nproduct has (no login, no accounts). A 401 tells anyone probing URLs \"this\nresource exists and requires authorization,\" which leaks exactly the\ninformation an unguessable link is supposed to withhold, and it invites the\nquestion \"authorization via what?\" in a product that has no auth flow to\nanswer it. Switching to 401 would be a real regression against the brief's\nown stated security model, not just a style preference.\n\nWhat actually changed, because the operator's underlying complaint (a blank\npage reads as \"the site is down\") is legitimate:\n- [server/src/server.js](server/src/server.js#L41) — the plain-text 404 body\n  is now `\"This link is invalid or has expired. Ask us for a new one.\"`\n  instead of `\"Not found.\"`\n- [web/src/pages/NotFoundPage.jsx](web/src/pages/NotFoundPage.jsx) — same\n  message on the client-rendered 404 page, so it doesn't read as breakage on\n  either code path that can produce it.\n\nStatus code unchanged; the actual UX problem (confusing, alarming copy) is\nfixed.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Timestamps — \"show 'last ingest' in our local time\"\n\n**DONE.**\n\n- [web/src/format.js](web/src/format.js#L20) — new `localDateTime()` formats\n  an ISO timestamp in a given IANA timezone via `Intl.DateTimeFormat`.\n- [web/src/pages/DashboardPage.jsx](web/src/pages/DashboardPage.jsx) — the\n  footer now renders `lastIngestAt` in the company's own timezone (e.g. *\"Aug\n  27, 2026, 8:50 AM GMT+8\"* for Fina/Manila, *\"Aug 26, 2026, 5:50 PM PDT\"* for\n  Lumen/LA), with the raw UTC ISO timestamp kept as a hover `title` for anyone\n  who wants it. An operator can now tell at a glance whether this morning's\n  local sync ran, which was the actual ask.\n\n---\n\n## 4. The negative spend row — \"delete it from the fixture before ingesting\"\n\n**CHANGED.**\n\nDid not hand-edit the fixture file, and did not drop the row. The row stays\nin `fixtures/fina.meta.ads.json` and stays in the ingested total.\n\nWhy deleting it is the wrong fix, not just a style choice:\n- **It's not corrupt data — the operator's own explanation confirms it's a\n  real, billed event.** \"Meta issued a credit\" means the company's Meta bill\n  for that day genuinely went down by ₱250. Deleting the row makes the\n  dashboard show ₱250 *more* spend than the company was actually charged —\n  the exact \"silently 'fixed' in a way that hides what really happened\"\n  failure mode `CANDIDATE-BRIEF.md`'s KPI contract explicitly rules out\n  (\"Rows the data can't support… must be visible somewhere — never silently\n  dropped, never silently 'fixed'\").\n- **Hand-editing a source fixture is not durable.** The fixture is meant to\n  stand in for a real upstream API response. The moment this file is\n  regenerated, re-synced, or replaced by a real Meta API integration, a\n  manual edit made once in this repo is gone and the \"credit shows up as a\n  weird row\" question resurfaces for whoever's on call then — with no record\n  that anyone ever investigated it the first time.\n- **There's no audit trail once it's deleted.** Today we know exactly which\n  campaign, which day, and how large the credit was. Silently deleting the\n  row (or silently zeroing it, which is the same failure by another name)\n  throws that information away.\n\nWhat we did instead\n([ads.js:86](server/src/ingest/ads.js#L86)): detect `spend < 0`, keep the row\nand let it reduce the day's total spend (that's economically correct — a\ncredit lowers what was actually spent), and flag it as an `ingest_issues` row\nwith reason `negative_spend_credit` so it's visible on the dashboard\n(`IssuesStrip.jsx`) instead of showing up as an unexplained dip nobody can\nexplain later. Proven in\n[server/test/fina_third_company.test.js](server/test/fina_third_company.test.js)\n(\"Fina's negative ad-spend row is a platform credit…\").\n"
}

1	# Northstar
2	
3	Slim commerce intelligence dashboards for two companies, built from their Shopify
4	order exports and Meta ad exports. See [`NOTES.md`](NOTES.md) for the full data-quality
5	review and design rationale, and [`CANDIDATE-BRIEF.md`](CANDIDATE-BRIEF.md) for the spec.
6	
7	This covers **Part A** (real schema, ingest, dashboards, tests), **Part B**
8	(scale ingest + latency), and **Part C** (ingest against a flaky HTTP source).
9	The day-2 change requests are not yet implemented.
10	
11	## Run it (one command)
12	
13	Requires Node 18+ and npm.
14	
15	```bash
16	npm install
17	npm start
18	```
19	
20	`npm start` runs the ingest pipeline against `fixtures/`, builds the React app, and
21	starts the server on `http://localhost:4000`. It prints both dashboard URLs when it's
22	ready. From a fresh clone this is under a minute (`npm install` is the only slow step).
23	
24	## Dashboards
25	
26	| Company   | URL |
27	|-----------|-----|
28	| Lumen Co  | http://localhost:4000/d/551c26ff3a0ccd4f85eb6f247d4053475525c8d7244f9604 |
29	| Harbor Co | http://localhost:4000/d/668f37f9758664de0943d2954ea73b123991b18ef441faf2 |
30	
31	An unknown or mistyped token returns a real HTTP 404, not a redirect and not the app
32	shell — try `http://localhost:4000/d/anything-else`.
33	
34	## Re-running ingest
35	
36	Ingest is idempotent — running it again against the same source files does not
37	change row counts or totals:
38	
39	```bash
40	npm run ingest
41	```
42	
43	See NOTES.md → "Idempotency proof" for the actual before/after output.
44	
45	## Part B — scale (300k orders/company)
46	
47	```bash
48	python3 tools/gen_scale_fixtures.py   # writes fixtures/scale/*.jsonl + EXPECTED.json
49	npm run ingest:scale                  # ingests into server/data/northstar.scale.sqlite
50	npm run bench                         # 90-day dashboard latency, 3 measurement tools
51	npm run serve:scale                   # serves the same two dashboard URLs off the scale DB
52	```
53	
54	`ingest:scale` prints wall-clock time and peak RSS. `bench` prints in-process
55	hrtime (cold + 30 warm calls) and `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` for every query the
56	dashboard runs. The dashboard's 90-day render is **~80ms server-side once the
57	SQLite connection is warm** (see `NOTES.md` → "Bottleneck" for the full
58	before/after, including why the very first request after a restart used to be
59	slower and how that's handled now).
60	
61	## Part C — failure handling (flaky source)
62	
63	```bash
64	python3 tools/flaky_source.py       # http://127.0.0.1:8787, injects 5xx/429/
65	                                     # truncated bodies/a duplicate page
66	npm run ingest:flaky                # ingests from the flaky source into
67	                                     # server/data/northstar.flaky.sqlite
68	npm run prove:kill-safety           # SIGKILLs ingest mid-run, proves no
69	                                     # half-written state, then re-ingests
70	                                     # to completion and diffs vs. Part A
71	```
72	
73	`ingest:flaky` finishes with exactly Part A's row counts and KPI totals —
74	see `NOTES.md` → "Part C — Failures" for the per-failure-mode handling and
75	the kill-safety proof.
76	
77	## Tests
78	
79	```bash
80	npm test
81	```
82	
83	22 tests, asserting numbers (not "a function was called") for duplicate order rows,
84	UTC-timestamp timezone conversion in both directions, refund netting and refund-date
85	attribution, a spend-with-zero-orders day, inclusive date-range boundaries,
86	wrong-currency ad exclusion, idempotent re-ingest, and — against the real
87	`tools/flaky_source.py`, not a mock — ingest reproducing Part A's exact totals
88	while actually triggering every injected failure mode.
89	
90	## KPI definitions (in my own words)
91	
92	All figures are in the company's own currency, bucketed by the **store-local**
93	calendar date (the company's IANA timezone, not UTC and not the server's clock).
94	
95	- **Gross sales** — sum of `quantity × price` across every line item of every real
96	  order placed that day, before any refund is applied.
97	- **Net revenue** — gross sales minus refunds issued that day. A refund is dated by
98	  when the refund itself happened, not when the original order was placed — a refund
99	  issued today against last week's order shows up in today's net, not last week's.
100	- **Orders** — count of distinct real orders. A refund is not a second order (the
101	  source data models it as a separate record, but it's an event against an existing
102	  order, not a new sale), and a byte-identical duplicate row from the export is not
103	  a second order either.
104	- **Ad spend** — sum of ad spend attributed to that store-local day, in the
105	  company's own currency. A resend of the exact same spend row doesn't double-count
106	  it; two genuinely different spend events for the same campaign on the same day
107	  both count. A row reported in a different currency than the company's is excluded
108	  from the total and flagged, not silently converted or dropped.
109	- **ROAS** — net revenue ÷ ad spend for the day (or the range). Shown as an em dash
110	  when spend is 0 for that period, rather than a divide-by-zero or a blank.
111	
112	## Architecture
113	
114	- `server/` — Node/Express API + ingest pipeline, SQLite (`better-sqlite3`) storage.
115	- `web/` — React (Vite) single-page app, one route: `/d/:token`.
116	- Root `package.json` uses npm workspaces so a single `npm install` covers both.
117	
118	## What's not built yet
119	
120	The day-2 change requests (`changes.zip`) are out of scope for this pass and not
121	implemented.
122	
123	The brief's commit-rule spacing requirement — at least two sessions 8+ hours apart —
124	is a real-world constraint on when these commits land, not something a single
125	implementation pass can satisfy; that's on the actual submission timeline, not the code.
126

  "new_string": "- **Orders** — count of distinct real orders. A refund is not a second order (the\n  source data models it as a separate record, but it's an event against an existing\n  order, not a new sale), a byte-identical duplicate row from the export is not\n  a second order either, and a **voided** order (payment authorization voided,\n  nothing captured) is not a sale.\n- **Ad spend** — sum of ad spend attributed to that store-local day, in the\n  company's own currency. A resend of the exact same spend row doesn't double-count\n  it; two genuinely different spend events for the same campaign on the same day\n  both count; a real platform-issued credit (negative spend) reduces the total,\n  since that's what the company was actually billed. A row reported in a different\n  currency than the company's is excluded from the total and flagged, not silently\n  converted or dropped — and the same rule applies to an **order** in the wrong\n  currency, excluded from gross/net/order-count and shown separately.\n- **ROAS** — net revenue ÷ ad spend for the day (or the range). Shown as an em dash\n  when spend is 0 for that period, rather than a divide-by-zero or a blank.\n- **Comparison strip** — the last day of the selected range vs. the same weekday\n  one week earlier (both in the company's own timezone), for net revenue, orders,\n  and ad spend. If either day has no data at all, the dashboard says so instead of\n  a comparison; if a day has data but a metric's baseline is legitimately zero, that\n  one metric shows \"—\" instead of a divide-by-zero or a misleading percentage."

+export function percent(value) {
+  if (value === null || value === undefined) return "—";
+  const sign = value > 0 ? "+" : "";
+  return `${sign}${value.toFixed(1)}%`;
+}
+
+// Renders an ISO instant in the company's own timezone -- CR2 #3: the "last
+// ingest" time was UTC-only, which doesn't tell an operator at a glance whether
+// this morning's local sync ran.
+export function localDateTime(isoTimestamp, timeZone) {
+  if (!isoTimestamp) return null;
+  // dateStyle/timeStyle can't be mixed with timeZoneName (Intl throws) -- spell
+  // out the fields instead so the timezone abbreviation still shows.
+  return new Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-US", {
+    timeZone,
+    year: "numeric",
+    month: "short",
+    day: "numeric",
+    hour: "numeric",
+    minute: "2-digit",
+    timeZoneName: "short",
+  }).format(new Date(isoTimestamp));
+}

+// Pure calendar-date arithmetic (no timezone conversion) -- store_local_date is
+// already a company-local bucket, so "one week earlier" is just subtracting 7
+// from that string, the same way enumerateDates walks the range.
+function daysBefore(dateStr, n) {
+  const d = new Date(`${dateStr}T00:00:00Z`);
+  return new Date(d.getTime() - n * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000).toISOString().slice(0, 10);

+// orders, and ad spend at a time, using the exact same currency/voided filters
+// as the range KPIs above -- otherwise the comparison and the daily table could
+// disagree on what a "sale" is. `hasData` is a *separate* signal (any row at all
+// recorded for the company on that date, filtered or not) from "the filtered
+// totals are zero" -- a day before the shop existed and a day where every order
+// was voided both zero out the same way, but only the first is "no data".
+function getSingleDayMetrics(db, company, date) {
+  const gross = db
+    .prepare(
+      `SELECT SUM(li.price_cents * li.quantity) AS cents
+       FROM line_items li JOIN orders o ON o.id = li.order_id
+       WHERE o.company_id = ? AND o.currency = ? AND o.financial_status != 'voided' AND o.store_local_date = ?`
+    )
+    .get(company.id, company.currency, date).cents ?? 0;
+
+  const orders = db
+    .prepare(
+      `SELECT COUNT(*) AS cnt FROM orders
+       WHERE company_id = ? AND currency = ? AND financial_status != 'voided' AND store_local_date = ?`
+    )
+    .get(company.id, company.currency, date).cnt;
+
+  const refunds = db
+    .prepare(
+      `SELECT SUM(r.amount_cents) AS cents
+       FROM refunds r LEFT JOIN orders o ON o.id = r.order_id
+       WHERE r.company_id = ? AND (o.id IS NULL OR (o.currency = ? AND o.financial_status != 'voided'))
+         AND r.store_local_date = ?`
+    )
+    .get(company.id, company.currency, date).cents ?? 0;
+
+  const spend = db
+    .prepare(
+      `SELECT SUM(spend_cents) AS cents FROM ad_spend WHERE company_id = ? AND currency = ? AND store_local_date = ?`
+    )
+    .get(company.id, company.currency, date).cents ?? 0;
+
+  const anyRow = db
+    .prepare(
+      `SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM orders WHERE company_id = ? AND store_local_date = ?
+         UNION SELECT 1 FROM ad_spend WHERE company_id = ? AND store_local_date = ?) AS present`
+    )
+    .get(company.id, date, company.id, date).present;
+
+  const netCents = gross - refunds;
+  return {
+    date,
+    netRevenue: centsToNumber(netCents),
+    orders,
+    adSpend: centsToNumber(spend),
+    hasData: Boolean(anyRow),
+  };
+}
+
+// null when the baseline is 0 (or missing) -- a percentage against a zero or
+// absent base is meaningless/divides-by-zero, not "misleadingly small". The UI
+// renders that as "—", same convention as ROAS with zero spend.
+function percentChange(current, previous) {
+  if (!previous) return null;
+  return ((current - previous) / Math.abs(previous)) * 100;
+}
+
+function getComparison(db, company, endDate) {
+  const previousDate = daysBefore(endDate, 7);
+  const current = getSingleDayMetrics(db, company, endDate);
+  const previous = getSingleDayMetrics(db, company, previousDate);
+
+  return {
+    current,
+    previous,
+    changes:
+      current.hasData && previous.hasData
+        ? {
+            netRevenue: percentChange(current.netRevenue, previous.netRevenue),
+            orders: percentChange(current.orders, previous.orders),
+            adSpend: percentChange(current.adSpend, previous.adSpend),
+          }
+        : null,
+  };
+}
+
 // Every calendar day in [start, end] gets a row, zero-filled -- not just the days
 // that happen to have an order or a spend record. This is what makes a day with ad
 // spend and zero orders show up correctly (NOTES.md defect #7), rather than only
@@ -28,32 +118,46 @@ export function getCompanyByToken(db, token) {
 export function getDashboardData(db, company, { start, end }) {
   const dates = enumerateDates(start, end);
 
+  // Orders in the wrong currency (Fina's F-308, a $50 USD order in a PHP company)
+  // and voided orders (F-305 -- authorization voided, nothing ever captured) are
+  // excluded from every sales KPI the same way ad_spend already excludes foreign-
+  // currency rows below: visible in ingest_issues / excludedForeignRevenue, never
+  // silently summed as if they were real store-currency sales.
   const grossRows = db
     .prepare(

        GROUP BY o.store_local_date`
     )
-    .all(company.id, start, end);
+    .all(company.id, company.currency, start, end);
 
   const orderCountRows = db

1	import crypto from "node:crypto";
2	import { decimalStringToCents } from "../money.js";
3	import { toStoreLocalDate, hasExplicitOffset } from "../timezone.js";
4	
5	// A byte-identical resend must be dropped; two genuinely different spend events for
6	// the same campaign/day must both be summed (NOTES.md defect #4 -- the starter's
7	// (campaign, date) key conflates the two). We dedupe on a hash of the full row
8	// instead, so identity is "this exact spend event", not "this campaign on this day".
9	function rowHash(rec) {
10	  const canonical = JSON.stringify({
11	    campaign_id: rec.campaign_id,
12	    date: rec.date,
13	    date_start: rec.date_start,
14	    spend: rec.spend,
15	    currency: rec.currency,
16	    impressions: rec.impressions,
17	    clicks: rec.clicks,
18	  });
19	  return crypto.createHash("sha256").update(canonical).digest("hex");
20	}
21	
22	export function ingestAds(db, company, records) {
23	  const stats = { rowsUpserted: 0, rowsSkippedDuplicate: 0, currencyMismatches: 0, issues: 0 };
24	  const ingestedAt = new Date().toISOString();
25	
26	  const insertAd = db.prepare(`
27	    INSERT INTO ad_spend (company_id, campaign_id, campaign_name, store_local_date, date_start_utc, spend_cents, currency, impressions, clicks, row_hash, ingested_at)
28	    VALUES (@companyId, @campaignId, @campaignName, @storeLocalDate, @dateStartUtc, @spendCents, @currency, @impressions, @clicks, @rowHash, @ingestedAt)
29	    ON CONFLICT(company_id, row_hash) DO NOTHING
30	  `);
31	  const recordIssue = db.prepare(`
32	    INSERT INTO ingest_issues (company_id, source, source_record_id, reason, detail, detected_at)
33	    VALUES (@companyId, 'ads', @sourceRecordId, @reason, @detail, @detectedAt)
34	    ON CONFLICT(company_id, source, source_record_id, reason) DO UPDATE SET
35	      detail = excluded.detail, detected_at = excluded.detected_at
36	  `);
37	
38	  const run = db.transaction((records) => {
39	    for (const rec of records) {
40	      if (!rec.date_start) {
41	        recordIssue.run({
42	          companyId: company.id,
43	          sourceRecordId: rec.campaign_id ?? null,
44	          reason: "missing_date_start",
45	          detail: "ad row missing date_start",
46	          detectedAt: ingestedAt,
47	        });
48	        stats.issues++;
49	        continue;
50	      }
51	
52	      if (!hasExplicitOffset(rec.date_start)) {
53	        recordIssue.run({
54	          companyId: company.id,
55	          sourceRecordId: rec.campaign_id ?? null,
56	          reason: "ambiguous_timestamp",
57	          detail: `ad row date_start "${rec.date_start}" has no UTC offset or Z -- true instant is undeterminable, row not ingested`,
58	          detectedAt: ingestedAt,
59	        });
60	        stats.issues++;
61	        continue;
62	      }
63	
64	      const storeLocalDate = toStoreLocalDate(rec.date_start, company.timezone);
65	      const hash = rowHash(rec);
66	      const result = insertAd.run({
67	        companyId: company.id,
68	        campaignId: rec.campaign_id,
69	        campaignName: rec.campaign_name ?? null,
70	        storeLocalDate,
71	        dateStartUtc: rec.date_start,
72	        spendCents: decimalStringToCents(rec.spend),
73	        currency: rec.currency ?? company.currency,
74	        impressions: rec.impressions ?? null,
75	        clicks: rec.clicks ?? null,
76	        rowHash: hash,
77	        ingestedAt,
78	      });
79	
80	      if (result.changes === 0) {
81	        stats.rowsSkippedDuplicate++;
82	        continue;
83	      }
84	      stats.rowsUpserted++;
85	
86	      // Negative spend is a real platform-issued credit (Meta refunding an
87	      // over-charge), not corrupt data -- it genuinely reduces what the company
88	      // was billed that day, so it stays in the sum (CR2-RESPONSE.md #4: we do
89	      // NOT hand-delete it from the fixture, which would silently erase the
90	      // audit trail and re-break the moment the fixture is re-synced). Flagged
91	      // here so it's visible on the dashboard rather than an unexplained dip.
92	      if (decimalStringToCents(rec.spend) < 0) {
93	        recordIssue.run({
94	          companyId: company.id,
95	          sourceRecordId: `${rec.campaign_id}:${rec.date_start}`,
96	          reason: "negative_spend_credit",
97	          detail: `ad row for ${rec.campaign_id} on ${storeLocalDate} has negative spend (${rec.spend} ${rec.currency ?? company.currency}) -- treated as a platform credit and included in the total, not deleted`,
98	          detectedAt: ingestedAt,
99	        });
100	        stats.issues++;
101	      }
102	
103	      if (rec.currency && rec.currency !== company.currency) {
104	        stats.currencyMismatches++;
105	        recordIssue.run({
106	          companyId: company.id,
107	          sourceRecordId: `${rec.campaign_id}:${rec.date}`,
108	          reason: "currency_mismatch",
109	          detail: `ad row currency ${rec.currency} does not match company currency ${company.currency}; excluded from spend total`,
110	          detectedAt: ingestedAt,
111	        });
112	      }
113	    }
114	  });
115	
116	  run(records);
117	  return stats;
118	}

1	import express from "express";
2	import { fileURLToPath, pathToFileURL } from "node:url";
3	import { dirname, join } from "node:path";
4	import { existsSync } from "node:fs";
5	import { openDb } from "./db.js";
6	import { getCompanyByToken, getDashboardData } from "./kpi.js";
7	import { COMPANIES, DEFAULT_RANGE, lastNDaysRange } from "./companies.js";
8	
9	const __dirname = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
10	const WEB_DIST = join(__dirname, "..", "..", "web", "dist");
11	
12	export function createApp(db) {
13	  const app = express();
14	
15	  if (existsSync(WEB_DIST)) {
16	    // index: false -- "/" must hit our own handler below, not auto-serve the SPA
17	    // shell (which is only valid under a checked /d/:token route).
18	    app.use(express.static(WEB_DIST, { index: false }));
19	  }
20	
21	  // Wrong or unknown token -> a real 404, not a redirect and not the SPA shell.
22	  app.get("/api/dashboard/:token", (req, res) => {
23	    const company = getCompanyByToken(db, req.params.token);
24	    if (!company) {
25	      return res.status(404).json({ error: "not_found" });
26	    }
27	    const start = req.query.start || DEFAULT_RANGE.start;
28	    const end = req.query.end || DEFAULT_RANGE.end;
29	    // Server-Timing exposes the same server-side render time the Part B budget
30	    // is measured against directly in a normal HTTP response, so `curl -w` or
31	    // browser devtools can see it without an in-process harness.
32	    const t0 = process.hrtime.bigint();
33	    const data = getDashboardData(db, company, { start, end });
34	    const renderMs = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - t0) / 1e6;
35	    res.set("Server-Timing", `dashboard;dur=${renderMs.toFixed(2)}`);
36	    res.json(data);
37	  });
38	
39	  app.get("/d/:token", (req, res) => {
40	    const company = getCompanyByToken(db, req.params.token);
41	    if (!company) {
42	      // CR2 #2: kept the 404 (see web/src/pages/NotFoundPage.jsx for why a 401
43	      // would leak information an unguessable link is meant to withhold) but
44	      // gave it copy that doesn't read as "the site is down".
45	      return res.status(404).send("This link is invalid or has expired. Ask us for a new one.");
46	    }
47	    const indexPath = join(WEB_DIST, "index.html");
48	    if (!existsSync(indexPath)) {
49	      return res.status(500).send("Web app is not built yet — run `npm run build`.");
50	    }
51	    res.sendFile(indexPath);
52	  });
53	
54	  app.get("/", (_req, res) => {
55	    res.send("Northstar — open your dashboard link (/d/{token}).");
56	  });
57	
58	  return app;
59	}
60	
61	// A cold SQLite connection's first hit on the dashboard's line_items JOIN costs
62	// ~500ms (page-in + b-tree decode on first touch of each page it needs) --
63	// index seeks already confirmed via EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN, so this isn't a missing-
64	// index problem. The server holds one long-lived connection for its whole life
65	// (see below), so that cost would otherwise land on whichever real request
66	// happens to be first. Pay it once at boot instead, before traffic arrives.
67	// See NOTES.md -> "Bottleneck" for the before/after measurements.
68	function warmDashboardCache(db) {
69	  const range = lastNDaysRange(DEFAULT_RANGE.end, 90);
70	  const t0 = process.hrtime.bigint();
71	  for (const cfg of Object.values(COMPANIES)) {
72	    const company = db.prepare(`SELECT * FROM companies WHERE slug = ?`).get(cfg.slug);
73	    if (company) getDashboardData(db, company, range);
74	  }
75	  const ms = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - t0) / 1e6;
76	  console.log(`Warmed dashboard cache for ${range.start}..${range.end} in ${ms.toFixed(1)}ms`);
77	}
78	
79	if (process.argv[1] && import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(process.argv[1]).href) {
80	  const db = openDb();
81	  const app = createApp(db);
82	  warmDashboardCache(db);
83	  const port = process.env.PORT || 4000;
84	  app.listen(port, () => {
85	    console.log(`Northstar server listening on http://localhost:${port}`);
86	  });
87	}
88

A plain in-memory flag\n  // is enough here -- this is a single-process app with one long-lived DB\n  // connection, so the only real risk is two ingestAll() calls racing each\n  // other's transactions, not a distributed-lock problem.\n  let ingesting = false;\n\n  if (existsSync(WEB_DIST)) {\n    // index: false -- \"/\" must hit our own handler below, not auto-serve the SPA\n    // shell (which is only valid under a checked /d/:token route).\n    app.use(express.static(WEB_DIST, { index: false }));\n  }\n\n  // Re-ingests from whichever source NORTHSTAR_SOURCE/NORTHSTAR_FIXTURES\n  // currently point at, against the running server's own DB connection --\n  // same ingestAll() the CLI (ingest/run.js) uses, no process restart. A\n  // second call while one is in flight gets a 409 instead of racing it.\n  app.post(\"/api/ingest\", async (_req, res) => {\n    if (ingesting) {\n      return res.status(409).json({ error: \"ingest_in_progress\" });\n    }\n    ingesting = true;\n    const t0 = process.hrtime.bigint();\n    try {\n      const result = await ingestAll(db);\n      const wallMs = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - t0) / 1e6;\n      res.json({ ...result, wallMs });\n    } catch (err) {\n      res.status(500).json({ error: \"ingest_failed\", message: String(err) });\n    } finally {\n      ingesting = false;\n    }\n  });"

1	import { useEffect, useState } from "react";
2	import { useParams } from "react-router-dom";
3	import { fetchDashboard } from "../api.js";
4	import KpiCards from "../components/KpiCards.jsx";
5	import DailyTable from "../components/DailyTable.jsx";
6	import DailyChart from "../components/DailyChart.jsx";
7	import IssuesStrip from "../components/IssuesStrip.jsx";
8	import ComparisonStrip from "../components/ComparisonStrip.jsx";
9	import NotFoundPage from "./NotFoundPage.jsx";
10	import { localDateTime } from "../format.js";
11	
12	export default function DashboardPage() {
13	  const { token } = useParams();
14	  const [data, setData] = useState(null);
15	  const [notFound, setNotFound] = useState(false);
16	  const [error, setError] = useState(null);
17	
18	  useEffect(() => {
19	    let cancelled = false;
20	    setData(null);
21	    setNotFound(false);
22	    setError(null);
23	
24	    fetchDashboard(token)
25	      .then((d) => {
26	        if (!cancelled) setData(d);
27	      })
28	      .catch((err) => {
29	        if (cancelled) return;
30	        if (err.status === 404) setNotFound(true);
31	        else setError(err.message);
32	      });
33	
34	    return () => {
35	      cancelled = true;
36	    };
37	  }, [token]);
38	
39	  if (notFound) return <NotFoundPage />;
40	  if (error) return <div className="page-center">Something went wrong: {error}</div>;
41	  if (!data) return <div className="page-center">Loading…</div>;
42	
43	  const { company, range, daily, totals, lastIngestAt, issues, excludedForeignSpend, excludedForeignRevenue, comparison } =
44	    data;
45	  const localIngestAt = lastIngestAt ? localDateTime(lastIngestAt, company.timezone) : null;
46	
47	  return (
48	    <div className="dashboard">
49	      <header className="dashboard-header">
50	        <h1>{company.name}</h1>
51	        <div className="dashboard-meta">
52	          <span>{company.currency}</span>
53	          <span>{company.timezone}</span>
54	          <span>
55	            {range.start} – {range.end}
56	          </span>
57	        </div>
58	      </header>
59	
60	      <KpiCards totals={totals} currency={company.currency} />
61	
62	      <ComparisonStrip comparison={comparison} currency={company.currency} />
63	
64	      <section className="chart-section">
65	        <h2>Daily</h2>
66	        <DailyChart daily={daily} currency={company.currency} />
67	      </section>
68	
69	      <section>
70	        <DailyTable daily={daily} currency={company.currency} />
71	      </section>
72	
73	      <IssuesStrip
74	        issues={issues}
75	        excludedForeignSpend={excludedForeignSpend}
76	        excludedForeignRevenue={excludedForeignRevenue}
77	        currency={company.currency}
78	      />
79	
80	      <footer className="dashboard-footer" title={lastIngestAt ? `${lastIngestAt} UTC` : undefined}>
81	        Last successful ingest: {localIngestAt ?? "never"}
82	      </footer>
83	    </div>
84	  );
85	}

  right behavior for a demo/take-home; in production I'd rather a company
  onboarding to a new source show up as a first-class "not yet backfilled"

  clause fragment rather than a shared view/CTE. Fine at this scale; the

Fine at this scale; the\n  third or fourth caller is where I'd factor it out.\n\n---\n\n## Stretch — `POST` re-ingest without a restart\n\nPicked this over incremental `sync_state` or a source-health strip because it\ndirectly serves the brief's own required deliverable: the walkthrough video\nneeds \"one re-ingest run,\" and a button that re-ingests the live server\nwithout a restart is a better demo of \"this actually works end to end\" than\na terminal screen recording of `npm run ingest`.\n\n[`server/src/server.js`](server/src/server.js#L21) adds\n`POST /api/ingest`, which calls the exact same `ingestAll(db)` the CLI\n(`ingest/run.js`) uses -- same idempotent upserts, same transaction\nboundaries per company/source, same kill-safety guarantees from Part C --\njust against the server process's own already-open, long-lived DB\nconnection instead of a fresh one. No caching layer needed invalidating:\n`getDashboardData` already recomputes straight from SQLite on every request\n([kpi.js:118](server/src/kpi.js#L118)), so a re-ingest's effects are visible\non the very next dashboard fetch. Every dashboard also has a **\"Re-ingest\nnow\"** button ([DashboardPage.jsx](web/src/pages/DashboardPage.jsx)) that\ncalls the endpoint and refetches itself.\n\nTwo things worth calling out:\n\n- **Concurrency.** This is a single-process app with one shared DB\n  connection -- two `ingestAll()` calls running at once would interleave\n  transactions against the same connection, which `better-sqlite3`'s\n  synchronous API doesn't protect against on its own. A plain in-memory\n  `ingesting` boolean set for the duration of the call\n  ([server.js:22](server/src/server.js#L22)) is enough for a single-process\n  app; a second `POST` while one is in flight gets `409` instead of racing\n  it. This would need a real lock (e.g. `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` or an external\n  mutex) the moment this ran as more than one process.\n- **What I didn't test.** The 409 path is exercised in\n  [reingest_endpoint.test.js](server/test/reingest_endpoint.test.js), but\n  against the tiny hand-authored fixtures ingest finishes in ~20-40ms, so two\n  concurrent `POST`s racing to actually collide on the lock (vs. both\n  finishing before either checks the other) is inherently timing-dependent\n  -- the test asserts the response pair is always `(200,200)` or `(200,409)`\n  and never anything else, rather than forcing the race to land a specific\n  way. Proving the lock actually *fires* deterministically would need an\n  injectable delay inside `ingestAll` (e.g. a hook only enabled under test),\n  which felt like more surface area than a stretch item warranted."

================================================================================
#575 USER  [2026-08-27T01:08:11.367Z]  (source line 971)
================================================================================
[tool_result]
- **Concurrency.** This is a single-process app with one shared DB
  connection -- two `ingestAll()` calls running at once would interleave
  transactions against the same connection, which `better-sqlite3`'s
  synchronous API doesn't protect against on its own. A plain in-memory
  `ingesting` boolean set for the duration of the call
  ([server.js:22](server/src/server.js#L22)) is enough for a single-process
  app; a second `POST` while one is in flight gets `409` instead of racing
  it. This would need a real lock (e.g. `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` or an external
  mutex) the moment this ran as more than one process.
- **What I didn't test.** The 409 path is exercised in
  [reingest_endpoint.test.js](server/test/reingest_endpoint.test.js), but
  against the tiny hand-authored fixtures ingest finishes in ~20-40ms, so two
  concurrent `POST`s racing to actually collide on the lock (vs. both
  finishing before either checks the other) is inherently timing-dependent
  -- the test asserts the response pair is always `(200,200)` or `(200,409)`
  and never anything else, rather than forcing the race to land a specific
  way. Proving the lock actually *fires* deterministically would need an
  injectable delay inside `ingestAll` (e.g. a hook only enabled under test),
  which felt like more surface area than a stretch item warranted.

================================================================================

## Findings

I did an exhaustive pass: extracted every actual human-typed message (not tool output) across all three transcripts by parsing the `#N USER [...] (source line N)` block structure, then grepped independently for pushback/correction language, plan-rejection signals, `AskUserQuestion` exchanges, and phrases like "good catch"/"you're right" that would mark the AI responding to a correction.

**Bottom line up front: these transcripts contain almost no live human back-and-forth.** Each session is essentially one long autonomous agent run kicked off by a single large task prompt, with only 2-4 short human messages total per session (mostly "commit this," "push it," "export this"). I found exactly **one substantive multi-turn redirect** and **one borderline "justify a choice" moment**. I could not find 5-8 genuine candidates — I'd say so plainly in AI-MOMENTS.md rather than padding it with weak matches.

### 1. Strong candidate — AI wrongly claims it has no way to export the session; human catches it and is proven right

**File:** session-03.txt, **lines ~3170–3300** (spans several turns)

- Line 3170: Human: "please export this in .txt file this sezsion"
- Line 3180: AI claims: "I can't run `/export` myself — it's a Claude Code CLI slash command... not a tool available to me in this session," telling the human to run it themselves.
- Line 3193: Human pushes back: "you we're able to do it last time so why not now? Please save this session as .txt file" — directly disputing the AI's claim of impossibility.
- Line 3226: AI (still resisting, blaming a typo/harness issue): "Good catch on my part being unclear... `/export` run by you is genuinely the only path."
- Line 3237: Human insists again, now pointing to concrete evidence: "so in the folder. the zip file is there. make it into .txt file."
- Lines 3243–3300: AI then runs `find`/`unzip`, discovers `session-export-1787750567714.zip` actually exists (containing `transcript.jsonl`), and proceeds to build `session-03.txt` from it — confirming the human was right and the AI's earlier refusal was wrong.

**Why it qualifies:** This is a clean "caught something the AI got wrong" + "redirected the AI when it went the wrong way" — the AI asserted a task was outside its ability, the human refused to accept that and pointed to concrete evidence (the zip file), and the AI reversed course and completed the task.

### 2. Weaker candidate — AI flags a risk and is made to lay out options before proceeding

**File:** session-02.txt, **lines ~1937–1965**

The AI notices the disk is at 100% capacity (1.6GB free) before generating Part B's scale fixtures, proactively raises the risk via `AskUserQuestion` ("How do you want to handle the tight disk space... before generating Part B's scale fixtures?") with three options (proceed at full scale / use smaller scale / pause to free space). Human selects "Proceed at full default scale (Recommended)" at line ~1965.

**Why it's weaker:** The AI raised this on its own initiative and the human simply picked the AI's own recommended option — there's no actual rejection or correction here, just a checkpoint. I'd only use this if you're short on stronger material and want to frame it as "I made the AI lay out the tradeoff explicitly before committing to a risky default," but it's a stretch for "made it justify a choice" since nothing was contested.

### What I did not find, despite specifically searching for it
- No plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`) was ever rejected or revised — the one plan in session-01 (line ~1834) was approved as-is.
- No numeric/KPI disagreement, no "that's wrong"/"no that's not right" exchange, no case of the AI saying "you're right, let me fix that" in response to a human correction, anywhere in any of the three files.
- The only other human messages in all three transcripts are single-line operational asks ("please commit this and push," "push it," "yes please. commit this.") — the AI complied without any correction being involved.

**My recommendation to the candidate:** Write AI-MOMENTS.md honestly with the one strong moment (#1 above, possibly split into two entries — "AI wrongly claimed the task was impossible" and "AI got corrected with evidence and reversed course" — if you want two of the 5-8 slots), note candidate #2 as a secondary if needed, and explicitly state that the remaining slots can't be filled from these three transcripts because the sessions were driven by single large prompts followed by long autonomous stretches with minimal live interaction — exactly the case the brief anticipates with "If you can't find five, say so and tell us why."

[tool_call: ExitPlanMode]
{
  "plan": "# Northstar Part A — real schema, React dashboards, one-command run\n\n## Context\n\n`northstar-takehome-v3/` currently has only the brief, fixtures, the (deliberately\nbroken) `starter/northstar_starter.py`, and a completed `NOTES.md` → \"Starter review\"\nthat catalogs 9 defects with exact proof records (dup orders, refund-as-order, UTC\ntimestamps sliced naively, ad resend-dedup collapsing genuine same-day spend, wrong-\ncurrency ad row, exclusive date range, spend-with-no-orders days dropped, undated\nrecords vanishing, float money). No application code exists yet.\n\nThe user wants Part A of the brief built now, specifically:\n1. A real ingest pipeline (orders, line items, refunds, ad rows as actual tables),\n   idempotent re-ingest, proven with a test/command output pasted into `NOTES.md`.\n2. Private per-company dashboards (`/d/{token}`) — company name, date range, the 5\n   KPIs, a daily table/chart, last-successful-ingest timestamp.\n3. Tests that pin down duplicates, timezone conversion, refunds, and a spend-no-orders\n   day — asserting numbers, not that a function ran.\n4. Stack constraint from the user: **React** frontend via **npm**, **SQLite** (local).\n5. One command to run the whole thing after `npm install`.\n6. Commits follow the brief's commit rules (≥10 commits, no commit >40% of final line\n   count, meaningful/incremental — the 2-day/8-hour spacing is on the user, not\n   fakeable in one sitting, so I'll flag that explicitly rather than pretend to satisfy it).\n\nEvery design choice below traces back to a specific defect in the Starter review so the\nKPI numbers in NOTES.md's \"Full corrected KPI table\" are exactly what the new pipeline\nmust reproduce — that table becomes the test oracle.\n\nPart B (scale/latency), Part C (flaky-source failure handling), and Day-2 (`changes.zip`)\nare explicitly out of scope for this pass.\n\n## Stack decisions\n\n- **Backend**: Node (already have v24.13, npm 11.6) + Express + `better-sqlite3`\n  (synchronous, mature, works on older Node too — safer for a reviewer's machine than\n  the experimental `node:sqlite`).\n- **Frontend**: React + Vite, `react-router-dom` for the `/d/:token` route. No chart\n  library — a small hand-rolled SVG bar/line component for the daily view, since the\n  brief only needs \"table and/or chart\" and it keeps deps light.\n- **Money**: integer minor units (cents), parsed from the source decimal strings —\n  avoids the float-drift bug (#9) without adding a bignum dependency.\n- **Timezone**: `Intl.DateTimeFormat` with `timeZone` to derive store-local\n  `YYYY-MM-DD` from the record's true instant — computed **once at ingest time** and\n  stored on the row (not recomputed per query), which both fixes bug #3 and sets up\n  Part B's latency target later without rework.\n- **Tests**: Node's built-in `node:test` + `node:assert/strict` — zero extra\n  dependency, asserts on real fixture numbers already verified by hand in NOTES.md.\n- **Monorepo**: npm workspaces (`server/`, `web/`) so `npm install` at the root pulls\n  both, and `npm start` chains ingest → web build → serve.\n\n## Schema (`server/src/migrate.js`)\n\n- `companies(id, slug, name, timezone, currency, dashboard_token UNIQUE)` — seeded\n  idempotently (upsert on slug) with two **fixed, pre-generated** random tokens\n  (checked into `companies.js` as config, not regenerated per run — otherwise the\n  README's dashboard links would go stale every re-ingest).\n- `orders(id, company_id, source_order_id, name, created_at_raw, store_local_date,\n  currency, financial_status, total_price_cents, UNIQUE(company_id, source_order_id))`\n  — **only real orders**. Records with `refund_of` set never land here.\n- `line_items(id, order_id FK, sku, title, quantity, price_cents)`\n- `refunds(id, company_id, source_refund_id, order_id FK NULL, store_local_date,\n  amount_cents, UNIQUE(company_id, source_refund_id))` — one row per refund record,\n  dated by the **refund's own** `created_at`, amount = `total_refunded` (so Harbor's\n  partial refund is $80, not the line-item sum of $90 — this is what fixes bug #1).\n- `ad_spend(id, company_id, campaign_id, campaign_name, store_local_date,\n  date_start_utc, spend_cents, currency, impressions, clicks, row_hash,\n  UNIQUE(company_id, row_hash))` — `row_hash` is a hash of every field on the source\n  row. A byte-identical resend hashes the same and is skipped (idempotent + fixes the\n  \"no dedup at all\" half of bug #2's sibling); two genuinely different rows for the\n  same campaign/day (bug #4's `L-C1` Aug 5 case) hash differently and both get summed.\n  `store_local_date` is computed from `date_start` via the same tz function used for\n  orders — the source's precomputed `date` field is not trusted as ground truth.\n- `ingest_issues(id, company_id, source, source_record_id, reason, detected_at,\n  UNIQUE(company_id, source, source_record_id, reason))` — every row the pipeline\n  can't cleanly use (missing `created_at`, wrong-currency ad row, orphan refund)\n  lands here instead of vanishing, satisfying \"must be visible somewhere.\"\n- `ingest_runs(id, started_at, finished_at, status, stats_json)` — drives the\n  dashboard's \"last successful ingest\" timestamp (shown in UTC, explicitly labeled —\n  CR2 later asks for local time; not preempting that here).\n- Indexes on `(company_id, store_local_date)` for orders, refunds, and ad_spend.\n\nIngest is idempotent via `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` (orders, keyed on\n`source_order_id`; refunds, keyed on `source_refund_id`) and `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`\nkeyed on content hash (ad_spend) / `(company, source, record, reason)` (issues) — so\nre-running never doubles a count. Every real order and duplicate source row shares the\nsame natural key, so a second ingest pass is a no-op on row counts and totals.\n\n## Ingest logic (`server/src/ingest/`)\n\n- `orders.js`: for each raw record — if `refund_of` is set, route to refunds\n  (amount = `total_refunded`, date = its own `created_at`); else if `created_at` is\n  missing, route to `ingest_issues` (`reason: \"missing_created_at\"`); else upsert into\n  `orders` + `line_items`. Byte-duplicate rows (`L-1007`, `H-204`) naturally collapse\n  on the `(company_id, source_order_id)` unique key.\n- `ads.js`: compute `store_local_date` from `date_start`; if `currency !==\n  company.currency`, still insert the row (so it's visible in issues + excludable from\n  spend) but flag it and exclude it from the spend aggregate; dedupe by row hash as above.\n- `run.js`: CLI entrypoint — ingests both sources for both companies, writes one\n  `ingest_runs` row, prints a per-company summary (orders / refunds / ad rows /\n  issues counts) to stdout. This stdout is what gets pasted into NOTES.md, run twice\n  in a row, to prove idempotency.\n\n## KPI query (`server/src/kpi.js`)\n\nFor a company + inclusive date range: generate **every calendar date in the range**\n(zero-filled, not a union of days-with-activity — this is the direct fix for bug #7,\nstronger than the starter's approach), left-join daily aggregates:\n- gross = `SUM(line_items.price_cents * quantity)` for real orders on that date\n- refunds = `SUM(refunds.amount_cents)` on that date\n- net = gross − refunds\n- orders = `COUNT(*)` from `orders` on that date\n- spend = `SUM(ad_spend.spend_cents)` where `currency = company.currency`, on that date\n- roas = `net / spend` when spend > 0, else `null` → UI renders \"—\"\n\n## API / server (`server/src/server.js`)\n\n- `GET /d/:token` — looks up the token server-side; unknown/wrong token → real HTTP\n  **404** (not the SPA shell), matching the brief's Part A spec (CR2 later asks for\n  401 — not implemented now). Valid token → serve the built SPA `index.html`.\n- `GET /api/dashboard/:token?start=&end=` — 404 JSON on bad token; otherwise company\n  info, KPI totals + daily rows, `last_ingest_at` (UTC), and the `ingest_issues`\n  summary for visibility. Defaults to `2026-08-01..2026-08-14`.\n- Serves `web/dist` as static assets for everything else.\n\n## Frontend (`web/src/`)\n\n- `App.jsx` — router: `/d/:token` → `DashboardPage`, everything else → simple not-found.\n- `DashboardPage.jsx` — fetches `/api/dashboard/:token`, renders company name + range,\n  5 KPI cards, a daily table, a small SVG chart (net revenue bars + spend line), the\n  \"last successful ingest\" timestamp, and a compact issues/flags strip (e.g. \"$25.00\n  USD ad spend excluded — currency mismatch, Aug 12\"; \"1 order excluded — missing\n  date\"). 404 response renders a plain \"this link is invalid\" state.\n\n## Tests (`server/test/`), against the **real fixtures**, asserting the exact numbers\nalready hand-verified in NOTES.md's corrected KPI table\n\n- `duplicates.test.js` — ingest Lumen/Harbor orders, assert `L-1007`/`H-204` each\n  produce exactly 1 order row (Harbor Aug 4 = 1 order, $240.00 gross).\n- `refunds.test.js` — Lumen Aug 4 net after `L-1003-R`; Harbor Aug 11 = **$160.00 net**\n  (not the starter's $250.00), and the refund lands on Aug 11 (its own date), not Aug 1.\n- `timezone.test.js` — `L-1009` buckets to Aug 4 (LA), `H-207` buckets to Aug 8\n  (Sydney), not the naive UTC-slice dates.\n- `spend_no_orders.test.js` — Lumen/Harbor Aug 9: 0 orders, spend present, day still\n  appears in the daily KPI rows with correct spend and ROAS = \"—\" logic upstream (net=0\n  but spend>0 → roas 0, not undefined — distinct from the spend=0 em-dash case).\n- `ad_dedup.test.js` — `L-C1` Aug 5 sums both rows to $150.90 (genuine two events), and\n  a re-ingest doesn't double it (idempotency on the hash key).\n- `currency_mismatch.test.js` — Harbor Aug 12 spend excludes `H-C9`'s $25 USD → $69.00,\n  and the row appears in `ingest_issues`.\n- `range.test.js` — Aug 14 is included (inclusive range fix for bug #6).\n- `idempotency.test.js` — run ingest twice against a temp DB; assert identical row\n  counts and identical KPI totals after each run. Output pasted into NOTES.md.\n- `totals.test.js` — full-range totals match NOTES.md exactly: Lumen 13 orders /\n  $1,750.00 / $192.00 refunds / $1,558.00 net / $771.15 spend / 2.02 ROAS; Harbor 14 /\n  $2,580.00 / $80.00 / $2,500.00 / $1,076.10 / 2.32 ROAS.\n\n## One-command run\n\nRoot `package.json` (npm workspaces: `server`, `web`):\n```\nnpm install        # once\nnpm start           # migrate + ingest + build web + serve on http://localhost:4000\n```\n`npm start` = `npm run ingest --workspace server && npm run build --workspace web && npm run serve --workspace server`.
